.

Applying the functor
\mathrm{Act}:\mathrm{GrMod}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Lemma
2.3
yields a functor
D_{p}=\mathrm{Act}(\phi_{p},f_{p}):\mathcal{D}_{u}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
.

Boundary stratifications:
For each boundary
k
-stratum
s
with associated
(k+1)
-stratum
t=\iota_{k}(s)
from (
23
), we have the bijections
\iota_{kj}:S^{loc}_{j}(s)\to S^{loc}_{j+1}(t)
from (
24
). This implies
M_{s}=M_{t}
and
G_{s}=G_{t}
as well as
f_{q}=f_{p}
and
\phi_{q}=\phi_{p}
for local boundary
l
-strata
p:s\to u
with associated local
(l+1)
-strata
q:t\to v
.

Combining these statements and formulating them in terms of the graded graph
Q^{X}
, the associated category
\mathcal{Q}^{X}
and the categories and functors from Definition
2.2
and Lemma
2.3
gives the following.

Lemma 4.3
.

An assignment of classical defect data to a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
defines
a functor
E^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{GrMod}
that assigns

•

to each
k
-stratum
u
of
X
the pair
(G_{u},M_{u})
defined by (
25
) and (
4
),

•

to each local
(k+1)
-stratum
p:u\to v
the pair
(\phi_{p},f_{p})
given by (
4
),

and a functor
D^{X}=\mathrm{Act}E^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
that assigns

•

to each
k
-stratum
u
of
X
the action groupoid
\mathcal{D}_{u}=\mathrm{Act}(G_{u},M_{u})=M_{u}\sslash G_{u}
,

•

to each local
(k+1)
-stratum
p:u\to v
the functor
D_{p}=\mathrm{Act}(\phi_{p},f_{p}):\mathcal{D}_{u}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
.

If
X
has a boundary, then
E^{\partial X}=E^{X}I_{\partial}
and
D^{\partial X}=D^{X}I_{\partial}
, where
I_{\partial}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathcal{Q}^{X}
is given in Lemma
3.23
.

The full assignment of defect data for a stratified
n
-manifold
X
is obtained by supplementing the classical defect data with representations of action groupoids and intertwiners between them.

Definition 4.4
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold.
An
assignment of defect data
to
X
assigns

•

to each
n
-stratum
t
a finite group
G_{t}
,

•

to each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
a finite
G_{L(s)}\times G_{R(s)}^{op}
-set
M_{s}
,

•

to each
(n-2)
-stratum
u
a representation
\rho_{u}:\mathcal{D}_{u}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
,

•

to each
(n-3)
-stratum
v
an intertwiner
\sigma_{v}:\rho_{v}^{t}\Rightarrow\rho_{v}^{s}
, where

\displaystyle\rho^{s}_{v}=\bigotimes_{s(e):v\to e}\,\rho_{e}D_{s(e)}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}\qquad\qquad\rho_{v}^{t}=\bigotimes_{t(e):v\to e}\,\rho_{e}D_{t(e)}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}.

(47)

The representations
\rho_{v}^{s}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
in (
47
) are constructed as follows. We consider all outgoing edge ends at
v
, or, equivalently, local 1-strata
s(e):v\to e
associated with starting ends of edges
e
at
v
. Applying
D^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
associates to each of them a functor
D_{s(e)}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}
. Post-composing it with the representation
\rho_{e}:\mathcal{D}_{e}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
yields a representation
\rho_{e}D_{s(e)}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
, and taking the tensor product of these representations yields the representation
\rho^{s}_{v}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
. The construction of the representation
\rho^{t}_{v}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
is analogous but considers the
incoming
instead of the
outgoing
edge ends at
v
.

Note that an assignment of defect data to a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
defines an assignment of defect data to
\partial X
. This allows one to extend the notion of a cobordism to stratified manifolds with defect data. Recall from Section
3.2.3
that embeddings and isomorphisms of stratified manifolds are required to respect the orientations of the manifolds and of all strata and the framings of the codimension 2-strata.
For stratified manifolds with defect data, we also require that they
respect the defect data:

Definition 4.5
.

Let
\Sigma_{0},\Sigma_{1}
be stratified surfaces with defect data.
A
defect cobordism
from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
is a compact stratified 3-manifold
M
with defect data, together with embeddings of stratified manifolds with defect data
\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}M\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
, that induces an isomorphism of stratified manifolds
\langle\iota_{0},\iota_{1}\rangle:\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}\to\partial M
.

Definition 4.6
.

The
defect cobordism category

\mathrm{Cob}^{\mathrm{def}}_{3}
is the symmetric monoidal category with

•

stratified surfaces with defect data as objects,

•

equivalence classes of defect cobordisms
\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}M\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
as morphisms from
\Sigma_{0}
to
\Sigma_{1}
, where
(M,\iota_{0},\iota_{1})\sim(M^{\prime},\iota^{\prime}_{0},\iota^{\prime}_{1})

if there is an isomorphism of stratified manifolds
\phi:M\to M^{\prime}
that respects the defect data
and makes the following diagram commute

\textstyle{M\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\textstyle{\overline{\Sigma}_{0}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{0}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}_{0}}
\textstyle{\Sigma_{1}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{1}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\prime}_{1}}
\textstyle{M^{\prime},}

•

composition of morphisms by gluing of stratified manifolds with defect data,

•

the identity morphism on
\Sigma
given by
\Sigma\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}\Sigma\times[0,1]\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma
,
where
\iota_{j}:\Sigma\to\Sigma\times[0,1]
,
x\mapsto(x,j)
,
\Sigma\times[0,1]
has a
(k+1)
-stratum
s\times[0,1]
for each
k
-stratum
s
of
\Sigma
, labeled by the same data and with the induced framing, if

s
is a stratum of codimension 2.

•

the symmetric monoidal structure given by disjoint unions.

Here and in the following,
\overline{\Sigma}
denotes the surface
\Sigma
with the opposite orientation. In the following, we suppress this orientation reversal when denoting a cobordism by
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}M\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
for better legibility.

Given this definition of the defect cobordism category, we define an oriented 3d defect TQFT as a symmetric monoidal functor from the defect cobordism category into the category of finite-dimensional vector spaces.

Definition 4.7
.

A 3d
defect TQFT
is a symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{Z}:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

We compare our definitions of the defect cobordism category and the resulting notion of a defect TQFT to other versions of defect cobordism categories
considered by Carqueville, Meusburger and Schaumann in
[
CMS
]
, by Carqueville, Runkel and Schaumann in
[
CRS1
,
CRS2
,
CRS3
]
, by Carqueville, Mulevičius, Runkel, Schaumann and Scherl
[
CMR+
]
and by Carqueville and Müller
[
CM
]
.
The first difference is that we work with stratified PL manifolds, while these publications consider
smooth strata in topological manifolds. We prefer to work in the PL setting, firstly because this is the setting used in
[
BMS
]
which is the basis of these constructions. More importantly, the constructions in Section
5.1
, are simpler and more intuitive in the PL setting. Performing them in the smooth setting would require more work and add technical complications.

The other substantial difference to the defect cobordism category used in
[
CMS
,
CRS1
,
CRS2
,
CRS3
,
CMR+
]
is the saturation condition on stratifications in Definition
3.7
, which is not imposed in those publications. Omitting the saturation condition leads to the appearance of defect points that are not contained in the closures of 1- or 2-strata and of defect lines that are not contained in the closures of 2-strata. This forces one to either artificially restrict the defect data associated to those points and lines or to
introduce additional framings by hand, see for instance the discussion on defect lines in
[
CRS1
, Remark 5.9 (ii)]
.

We feel that it is more natural to exclude such defect lines and points. Firstly, because this avoids the mentioned issues with the framings and restrictions on the defect data. Secondly, the algebraic data on the higher-dimensional strata that contain a given stratum in their closures define the source and target of the higher categorical data on this stratum. Finally, forbidding such strata does not restrict generality, as such strata can be realised by labelling the incident strata with
transparent defect data
, see Definition
7.1
.

Our framings from Definition
3.10
resemble the

*
-decorations considered in
[
CRS1
, Def. 5.4]
, but play a different role. As explained in Section
3.2.3
, we need the framing to ensure that the bundles of local strata of isolated loops are trivial. As the gluing of cobordisms respects the framing, 1-strata cannot glue to isolated loops with non-trivial bundles of local strata.
In contrast, the
*
-decorations in
[
CRS1
]
are used to consistently assign algebraic data to 1-strata
[
CRS1
, Remark 5.3]
. It is then shown that the resulting defect TQFT does not depend on the choice of these
*
-decorations, due to the algebraic properties of the defect data. We do not require the framing to assign defect data. However, we do not know if our formalism, in particular Theorem
6.1
, extends to isolated loops with non-trivial bundles of local strata.

5
Gauge configurations and gauge transformations

In this section, we associate to each stratified surface and each stratified cobordism with classical defect data a groupoid, the gauge groupoid of the surface or cobordism.
Its objects are called gauge configurations and its morphisms gauge transformations.
We show that this groupoid is an action groupoid.

This groupoid is constructed as follows. We describe each compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
by a graded graph
Q^{X}
as in Section
3.3

and the associated category
\mathcal{Q}^{X}
. We consider the functor
D^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Lemma
4.3

that encodes the algebraic content of the theory, the classical defect data.
In Section
5.1
we construct another functor,
T^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
, that encodes the topological content of the theory, fundamental groupoids associated to strata, and functors between them associated to local strata.
Gauge configurations and gauge transformations assign algebraic content to the topological content and are given by natural transformations from the functor
T^{X}
to the functor
D^{X}
and modifications between them.

To implement this, we introduce in Section
5.2
a category whose objects are triples of a graded graph
Q
and functors
D,T:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
and whose morphisms are given by graph maps and certain natural transformations. The gauge groupoid of a stratified
n
-manifold
X
is then defined and analysed in Section
5.3
. Sections
5.4
and
5.5
give a description of the gauge groupoids in terms of fundamental groupoids with basepoints and a fully combinatorial description for fine stratifications.

5.1
Fundamental groupoids associated with stratifications

We now associate to each compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
with associated graded graph
Q^{X}
a functor
B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Top}
and a functor
T^{X}=\Pi_{1}B^{X}:Q^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
. As these functors must also take into account the inclusions of lower-dimensional skeleta into higher-dimensional ones, it is not possible to work directly with the strata. Instead, we associate to each stratum
t
a compact PL manifold
\hat{t}
, whose interior coincides with
t
, and to each local stratum
q:t\to u
a PL map
\iota_{q}:\hat{t}\to\hat{u}
as follows.

Recall from Section
3.2
that each compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
has a triangulation
T
that contains all skeleta of
X
as subcomplexes. We fix such a triangulation
T
. When we consider neighbourhoods
U_{x}
as in Definition
3.7
, we always assume that they are chosen small enough such that each
k
-simplex of
T
intersects at most one
k
-stratum of
U_{x}
.

Spaces assigned to strata:
The spaces
\hat{t}
assigned to
k
-strata
t
are obtained by first cutting the
k
-skeleton of
X
along the
(k-1)
-skeleton.
More precisely, to construct
\hat{t}
, we
glue all
k
-simplexes
\tau
of
T
with
\tau\cap t\neq\emptyset
along those
(k-1)
-faces
\rho
with
\rho\cap t\neq\emptyset
.
This yields a compact PL manifold with boundary
\hat{t}
whose interior is PL homeomorphic to
t
. It is the geometric realisation of a finite simplicial complex.

Sending each
k
-simplex
\hat{\tau}
of
\hat{t}
to the associated
k
-simplex
\tau
of
t
yields a continuous map
f_{t}:\hat{t}\to\bar{t}
that is injective in the interior of the simplexes.
Neither the spaces
\hat{t}
nor the maps
f_{t}
depend on
T
.

Maps assigned to local strata:

This construction assigns to each local
l
-stratum
q:s\to t
a PL map
\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
as follows.
For any
k
-simplex
\sigma
of
\bar{s}
and any
x\in\mathring{\sigma}
we can choose a neighbourhood
U_{x}
as in Definition
3.7
, such that each
l
-simplex
\tau
of
\bar{t}
intersects at most one
l
-stratum of
U_{x}
. Let
\tau_{1},\ldots,\tau_{r}
be the
l
-simplexes of
\bar{t}
that contain
\sigma
as a
k
-face and satisfy
q^{\prime}\cap\tau_{j}\neq\emptyset
for
the unique

l
-stratum
q^{\prime}
of
U_{x}
representing
q
.
Denote by
\hat{\tau}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\tau}_{r}
the associated
l
-simplexes of
\hat{t}
with
f_{t}(\hat{\tau}_{j})=\tau_{j}
. The simplex
\sigma
corresponds to a unique
k
-simplex
\hat{\sigma}
of
\hat{s}
, and all
k
-faces
\hat{\rho}
of
\hat{\tau}_{1},\ldots,\hat{\tau}_{m}
with
f_{t}(\hat{\rho})=\sigma
correspond to a single
k
-simplex
\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
of
\hat{t}
.
The assignments
\hat{\sigma}\to\hat{\sigma}^{\prime}
then define a PL map
\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}

that is independent of
T
and such that the following diagram commutes for
the inclusions
\iota_{st}:\bar{s}\to\bar{t}

\textstyle{\hat{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{q}}
\textstyle{\hat{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{t}}
\textstyle{\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{st}}
\textstyle{\bar{t}.}

Boundary strata:

Let
s
be a
k
-stratum of
\partial X
and

t=\iota_{k}(s)
the
associated
(k+1)
-stratum of
X
from (
23
). Then for each
k
-simplex
\sigma

in
\partial X
with
\sigma\cap s\neq\emptyset
there is a unique

(k+1)
-simplex

\tau
in
X
with
\tau\cap t\neq\emptyset
and
\tau\cap\partial X=\sigma
. Denote by
\hat{\sigma}
and
\hat{\tau}
the associated simplexes in
\hat{s}
and
\hat{t}
. Sending
\hat{\sigma}
to the corresponding
k
-face of
\hat{\tau}
defines a PL embedding

\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
that does not depend on
T
.

For a local
l
-stratum
p:s\to u
of
s
and the associated local
(l+1)
-stratum
q:t\to v
at
t
from (
24
) this yields the
following commuting diagram, where the arrows in the lower quadrilateral are the obvious inclusions

\textstyle{\hat{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{u}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{u\partial}}
\textstyle{\hat{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{p}}
\scriptstyle{f_{s}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{s\partial}}
\textstyle{\hat{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{t}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{q}}
\textstyle{\hat{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{v}}
\textstyle{\bar{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{uv}}
\textstyle{\bar{s}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{su}}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{st}}
\textstyle{\bar{t}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{tv}}
\textstyle{\bar{v}.}

We now describe these constructions in terms of the graded graphs
Q^{X}
and
Q^{\partial X}
and the associated categories
\mathcal{Q}^{X}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}
.
The spaces
\hat{s}
associated to the strata and the maps
\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
associated to local strata
q:s\to t
define a functor
B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Top}
.
Likewise, the corresponding data for the boundary defines a functor
B^{\partial X}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathrm{Top}
.
The upper quadrilateral in the commuting diagram (
5.1
) states that the maps
\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
are natural with respect to the morphisms in
\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}
.
Denoting by
I_{\partial}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathcal{Q}^{X}
the functor from Lemma
3.23
induced by the insertion
i_{\partial}:Q^{\partial X}\to Q^{X}
, we then obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 5.1
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold.
There is a functor
B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Top}
that assigns

•

to a
k
-stratum
s
of
X
the space
\hat{s}
,

•

to a local
(k+1)
-stratum
q:s\to t
the map
\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
from (
5.1
).

Post-composition with
\Pi_{1}:\mathrm{Top}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
yields the functor
T^{X}=\Pi_{1}B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
that assigns

•

to a
k
-stratum
s
the groupoid
\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})
,

•

to a local
(k+1)
-stratum
q:s\to t
the functor
\Pi_{1}(\iota_{q}):\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})
.

The maps from (
5.1
) define natural transformations
\beta^{X}:B^{\partial X}\Rightarrow B^{X}I_{\partial}
and
\tau^{X}=\Pi_{1}\beta^{X}:T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow T^{X}I_{\partial}

with

\displaystyle\beta^{X}_{s}=\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t},

\displaystyle\tau^{X}_{s}=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{s\partial}):\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{t}).

(62)

5.2
Categorical description

We now introduce a category that encodes the graded graphs
Q^{X}
for compact stratified
n
-manifolds
X
and the associated functors
D^{X},T^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Lemma
4.3
and Proposition
5.1
. This category must take into account the relation between the data for
X
and the data for its boundary
\partial X
.
While the functors
D^{X}
and
D^{\partial X}=D^{X}I_{\partial}
are directly related by the functor
I_{\partial}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathcal{Q}^{X}
from Lemma
4.3
, the functors
T^{X}
and
T^{\partial X}
involve an additional natural transformation
\tau^{X}:T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow T^{X}I_{\partial}
given in (
62
). Thus, the morphisms in this category are given by graph maps, together with natural transformations.

Definition 5.2
.

We denote by
\mathrm{Pack}
the category whose

•

objects are triples
(Q,T,D)
of a graph
Q
and functors
T,D:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
,

•

morphisms from
(Q,T,D)
to
(Q^{\prime},T^{\prime},D^{\prime})
are pairs
(f,\tau)
of a graph map
f:Q\to Q^{\prime}
with associated functor
F:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}
satisfying
D=D^{\prime}F
, and a natural transformation
\tau\colon T\Rightarrow T^{\prime}F
,

•

composition of morphisms is given by
(f^{\prime},\tau^{\prime})\circ(f,\tau)=\big(f^{\prime}\circ f,(\tau^{\prime}F)\circ\tau\big)
.

Inserting functors
D,T:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
into the functor
\mathrm{GRPd}(-,-):\mathrm{Grpd}^{op}\times\mathrm{Grpd}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
yields a functor
\mathrm{GRPd}(T,D):\mathcal{Q}^{op}\times\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
.
By taking the ends of such functors we then obtain the following proposition.

Proposition 5.3
.

There is a functor
G:\mathrm{Pack}^{op}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
that assigns

•

to an object
\mathcal{P}=(Q,T,D)
the end of the functor
\mathrm{GRPd}(T,D):\mathcal{Q}^{op}\times\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}

G(\mathcal{P})=\int_{v\in Q_{0}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(v),D(v)\big),

•

to a morphism
\phi=(f,\tau):\mathcal{P}\to\mathcal{P}^{\prime}
the induced morphism
G(\phi):G(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\to G(\mathcal{P})
between ends.

Proof.

We write
D(u)^{T(v)}=\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(v),D(u)\big)

and denote the morphisms that characterise the ends by

e_{v}:G(\mathcal{P})\to D(v)^{T(v)}\qquad\qquad e^{\prime}_{w}:G(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\to D^{\prime}(w)^{T^{\prime}(w)}.

Then the functor
G(\phi):G(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\to G(\mathcal{P})
is induced by the universal property of the end via the following diagram that commutes for every morphism
h:u\to v
in
\mathcal{Q}

Functoriality follows by considering this for composites in
\mathrm{Pack}
and for
(\mathrm{id},\mathrm{id}):(Q,T,D)\to(Q,T,D)
.
∎

Remark 5.4
.

The functor
G(\phi):G(\mathcal{P}^{\prime})\to G(\mathcal{P})
for a morphism
\phi=(f,\tau):(Q,T,D)\to(Q^{\prime},T^{\prime},D^{\prime})
from diagram (
5.2
)
can be described equivalently as the following composite

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{\prime}(x^{\prime}),D^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\big)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\big(\mathrm{GRPd}(\hat{\tau},D^{\prime})\big)}

\displaystyle\int_{x^{\prime}\in\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(\mathrm{Lan}_{F}T(x^{\prime}),D^{\prime}(x^{\prime})\big)\cong\int_{x\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(x),D^{\prime}F(x)\big)=\int_{x\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(x),D(x)\big).

Here,
\hat{\tau}:\mathrm{Lan}_{F}T\Rightarrow T^{\prime}
is induced by

\tau:T\Rightarrow T^{\prime}F
via the universal property of the left Kan extension, and we applied Lemma
A.3
for
\mathbf{V}=\mathrm{Grpd}
.

We give a more explicit construction of the groupoid
G(\mathcal{P})
. Recall from Section
2.1
the interval groupoid
I
. Taking its tensor product with the identity functor with respect to the cartesian monoidal structure of
\mathrm{Grpd}
defines
a functor
(-)\times I:\mathrm{Grpd}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
. Its composites with a functor
T:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
and with a natural transformation
\tau:T\Rightarrow T^{\prime}
are denoted
T\times I=(-\times I)T:\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
and
\tau\times I=(-\times I)\tau:T\times I\Rightarrow T^{\prime}\times I
. The inclusions
\iota^{0}_{\mathcal{G}},\iota^{1}_{\mathcal{G}}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{G}\times I
for each groupoid
\mathcal{G}
define natural transformations
\iota^{0},\iota^{1}:T\Rightarrow T\times I
. Recall also from Section
2.1
that functors
F:\mathcal{G}\times I\to\mathcal{H}
correspond to natural transformations between the functors
F^{0}=F\iota^{0},F^{1}=F\iota^{1}:\mathcal{G}\to\mathcal{H}
. We thus denote them by the same letters.

Lemma 5.5
.

1.

The groupoid
G(Q,T,D)
for an object
(Q,T,D)
from Proposition
5.3
has

•

as objects natural transformations,
A\colon T\Rightarrow D
,

•

as morphisms
\gamma:A^{0}\Rrightarrow A^{1}
natural transformations
\gamma:T\times I\Rightarrow D
with
\gamma\circ\iota^{0}=A^{0}
and
\gamma\circ\iota^{1}=A^{1}

2.

The functor
G(f,\tau):G(Q^{\prime},T^{\prime},D^{\prime})\to G(Q,T,D)

for a morphism
(f,\tau):(Q,T,D)\to(Q^{\prime},T^{\prime},D^{\prime})
sends

•

a natural transformation
A^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\Rightarrow D^{\prime}

to the natural transformation
(A^{\prime}F)\circ\tau:T\Rightarrow D
,

•

a natural transformation
\gamma^{\prime}:T^{\prime}\times I\Rightarrow D^{\prime}

to
\gamma=(\gamma^{\prime}F)\circ(\tau\times I):T\times I\Rightarrow D
.

Proof.

1. The functor
{\mathrm{GRPd}(T,D)}:\mathcal{Q}^{op}\times\mathcal{Q}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
in Proposition
5.3
assigns to a pair of vertices
(v,v^{\prime})\in Q_{0}\times Q_{0}
the groupoid
\mathrm{Grpd}\big(T(v),D(v^{\prime})\big)=D(v^{\prime})^{T(v)}
with functors
A_{v,v^{\prime}}:T(v)\to D(v^{\prime})
as objects and natural transformations
\gamma_{v,v^{\prime}}:A^{0}_{v,v^{\prime}}\Rightarrow A^{1}_{v,v^{\prime}}
as morphisms from
A^{0}_{v,v^{\prime}}
to
A^{1}_{v,v^{\prime}}
.

Its end
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{GRPd}(T,D)
is the subgroupoid of the product
\prod_{v\in Q_{0}}D(v)^{T(v)}
that equalises post-composition with
D(h)
and pre-composition with
T(h)
for all edges
h:u\to v
in
Q
. As
\mathrm{Ob}:\mathrm{Grpd}\to\mathrm{Set}
preserves limits, objects of
\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{GRPd}(T,D)

are families
(A_{v})_{v\in Q_{0}}
of functors
A_{v}:T(v)\to D(v)
such that the diagram

\textstyle{T(u)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{u}}
\scriptstyle{T(h)}
\textstyle{T(v)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{v}}
\textstyle{D(u)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D(h)}
\textstyle{D(v)}

commutes for all edges
h:u\to v
in
Q
. This is a natural transformation
A:T\Rightarrow D
.

Morphisms from
A^{0}:T\Rightarrow D
to
A^{1}:T\Rightarrow D
are families
(\gamma_{v})_{v\in Q^{X}_{0}}
of natural transformations
\gamma_{v}:A^{0}_{v}\Rightarrow A^{1}_{v}
such that
D(h)\gamma_{u}=\gamma_{v}T(h)
for all edges
h:u\to v
. This is
a natural transformation
\gamma:T\times I\Rightarrow D
such that diagram (
•
‣
1
) commutes.

2. The morphisms
e_{v}:G(Q,T,D)\to D(v)^{T(v)}
that characterise the end assign to a natural transformation
A:T\Rightarrow D
the functor
A_{v}:T(v)\to D(v)
.
The commuting rectangles on top and on the left in diagram (
5.2
) then state that
(A^{\prime}F)_{v}\circ\tau_{v}=A_{v}
and
\gamma^{\prime}_{f(v)}\circ(\tau_{v}\times\mathrm{id}_{I})=\gamma_{v}
for
A=G(f,\tau)A^{\prime}
.
∎

Remark 5.6
.

We will use the concrete formulas from the proof of Lemma
5.5
. Note that the objects of the groupoid can be obtained more easily using that the functor
\mathrm{Ob}:\mathrm{Grpd}\to\mathrm{Set}
is a right adjoint:

\displaystyle\mathrm{Ob}\int_{x\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(x),D(x)\big)\cong\int_{x\in\mathcal{Q}}\mathrm{Ob}\,\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T(x),D(x)\big)=\int_{x\in\mathcal{Q}}\hom_{\mathrm{Grpd}}\big(T(x),D(x)\big)\cong{\rm Nat}(T,D).

5.3
The groupoid of gauge configurations and gauge transformations

A gauge configuration on a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
with defect data should relate the
topological content
of the stratification to its
algebraic
content.
The stratification is encoded in the graph
Q^{X}
from Definition
3.22
.
The topological content is encoded in the fundamental groupoids assigned to different strata and the functors between them. It is given by the functor
T^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Proposition
5.1
. The algebraic content is given by the defect data for the strata and encoded in the functor
D^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Lemma
4.3
.
The groupoid that relates this data was constructed in Proposition
5.3
and Lemma
5.5
.

Definition 5.7
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data.
The
gauge groupoid
on
X
, with
gauge configurations
as objects and
gauge transformations
as morphisms,
is the groupoid

G\big(Q^{X},T^{X},D^{X}\big):=\int_{s\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{X}(s),D^{X}(s)\big)=\int_{s\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})}.

For each stratum
t
of
X
the end defines a functor

\displaystyle P_{t}:\int_{s\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\mathcal{D}_{s}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})}\to\mathcal{D}_{t}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})}.

It follows
from Definition
5.7
that
gauge configurations and transformations on a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
restrict to gauge configurations and transformations on its boundary
\partial X
.
By Proposition
4.3
the defect data on
X
defines the defect data of
\partial X
and a functor

D^{\partial X}=D^{X}I_{\partial}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
, where
I_{\partial}:\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}\to\mathcal{Q}^{X}
is the functor from Lemma
3.23
.
Proposition
5.1
defines a natural transformation
\tau^{X}:T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow T^{X}I_{\partial}
.
Proposition
5.3
and Lemma
5.5
then imply the following, in the notation introduced before Lemma
5.5
.

Corollary 5.8
.

Let
X
be a stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data. There is a restriction functor

P_{\partial}:G(Q^{X},T^{X},D^{X})\to G(Q^{\partial X},T^{\partial X},D^{\partial X})

that assigns

•

to a gauge configuration
A^{X}:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
on
X
the boundary gauge configuration

A^{\partial X}=(A^{X}I_{\partial})\circ\tau^{X}:T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow D^{\partial X},

•

to a gauge transformation
\gamma^{X}:A^{X}\Rrightarrow A^{\prime X}
on
X
the boundary gauge transformation

\gamma^{\partial X}=\gamma^{X}I_{\partial}\circ(\tau^{X}\times I):A^{\partial X}\Rrightarrow A^{\prime\partial X}.

Remark 5.9
.

As in Remark
5.4
,
the functor
P_{\partial}

from Corollary
5.8
can also be constructed with ends as the following composite

\displaystyle G(Q^{X},T^{X},D^{X})

\displaystyle=\int_{t\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{X}(t),D^{X}(t)\big)\xrightarrow{\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\big(\mathrm{GRPd}(\hat{\tau}_{X},D^{X})\big)}\int_{t\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{GRPd}\big(\mathrm{Lan}_{I_{\partial}}T^{\partial X}(t),D^{X}(t)\big)

\displaystyle\cong\int_{s\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{\partial X}(s),D^{X}I_{\partial}(s)\big)=\int_{s\in\mathcal{Q}^{\partial X}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{\partial X}(s),D^{\partial X}(s))=G(Q^{\partial X},T^{\partial X},D^{\partial X}\big),

where we applied Lemma
A.3
for
\mathbf{V}=\mathrm{Grpd}
and
\hat{\tau}^{X}:\mathrm{Lan}_{I_{\partial}}T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow T^{X}
is induced by
\tau^{X}:T^{\partial X}\Rightarrow T^{X}I_{\partial}
via the universal property of the left Kan extension.

We now apply Lemma
5.5
and the characterisation of the functors
D^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Lemma
4.3
to
describe the gauge groupoid more concretely.

Lemma 5.10
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data.

1.

A gauge configuration on
X
is an assignment
of a map
h_{u}:\hat{u}\to M_{u}
and a functor
\psi_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\bullet\sslash G_{u}
to each
k
-stratum
u
such that for all paths
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{u}

\displaystyle h_{u}(y^{\prime})=\psi_{u}([\delta])\rhd h_{u}(y),

(79)

and the following diagrams commute for all local
(k+1)
-strata
q:u\to v

\textstyle{\hat{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{h_{u}}
\textstyle{M_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{f_{q}}
\textstyle{\hat{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{h_{v}}
\textstyle{M_{v}}

and

\textstyle{\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{q})}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{u}}
\textstyle{\bullet\sslash G_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{q}}
\textstyle{\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\psi_{v}}
\textstyle{\bullet\sslash G_{v}.}

2.

A gauge transformation
\gamma:A=(h,\psi)\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}=(h^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
is an assignment of a map
\gamma_{u}:\hat{u}\to G_{u}
to each
k
-stratum
u
such that
for all
y,y^{\prime}\in\hat{u}
and paths
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{u}

\displaystyle h^{\prime}_{u}(y)=\gamma_{u}(y)\rhd h_{u}(y)\qquad\psi^{\prime}_{u}([\delta])=\gamma_{u}(y^{\prime})\cdot\psi_{u}([\delta])\cdot\gamma_{u}(y)^{-1}

(88)

and the following diagram commutes for all local
(k+1)
-strata
q:u\to v

\textstyle{\hat{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota_{q}}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{u}}
\textstyle{G_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\phi_{q}}
\textstyle{\hat{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\gamma_{v}}
\textstyle{G_{v}.}

In particular, the groupoid
G(\mathcal{Q}^{X},T^{X},D^{X})=\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
is an action groupoid, where
\mathcal{A}^{X}
is the set of gauge transformations, and
\mathcal{G}^{X}
the
group of gauge transformations
, defined in the proof.

Proof.

1. By Lemma
5.5
a gauge configuration
A:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
is an assignment of a functor
A_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\mathcal{D}_{u}
to each stratum
u
of
X
such that the following diagram commutes for all local strata
q:u\to v

\textstyle{\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{q})}
\scriptstyle{A_{u}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{u}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{q}}
\textstyle{\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{A_{v}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{v}.}

Here,
\iota_{q}:\hat{u}\to\hat{v}
is the map from (
5.1
),
\mathcal{D}_{u}=\mathrm{Act}(G_{u},M_{u})=M_{u}\sslash G_{u}
is the action groupoid given by (
25
) and
D_{q}=\mathrm{Act}(\phi_{q},f_{q}):\mathcal{D}_{u}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
is the functor defined by (
4
) and Lemma
4.3
.

As
\mathcal{D}_{u}=M_{u}\sslash G_{u}
is an action groupoid,
a functor
A_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\mathcal{D}_{u}
is equivalent to the choice of a map
h_{u}:\hat{u}\to M_{u}
and a functor
\psi_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\bullet\sslash G_{u}
satisfying (
79
). Condition (
5.3
) translates into (
1
).

2. By the proof of Lemma
5.5
, a gauge transformation
\gamma:A=(h,\psi)\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}=(h^{\prime},\psi^{\prime})
is an assignment of a natural transformation
\gamma_{u}:A_{u}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{u}
to each
k
-stratum
u
of
x
such that
for all local
(k+1)
-strata
q:u\to v

\displaystyle D_{q}\gamma_{u}=\gamma_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{q}).

(97)

A natural transformation
\gamma_{u}:A_{u}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{u}
between functors
A_{u},A^{\prime}_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to M_{u}\sslash G_{u}
is equivalent to an assignment of a map
\gamma_{u}:\hat{u}\to G_{u}
to each
k
-stratum
u
that satisfies (
88
). Condition (
97
) translates into (
2
).

3. It is clear that the gauge configurations form a set
\mathcal{A}^{X}
. It follows from 2. that the gauge transformations form a group
\mathcal{G}^{X}
, with the group structure given by the pointwise multiplication of the maps
\gamma_{u}:\hat{u}\to G_{u}
. The action of
\mathcal{G}^{X}
on
\mathcal{A}^{X}
is given by (
88
), and this implies
G(Q^{X},T^{X},D^{X})=\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
.
More explicitly, the group of gauge transformations is
\mathcal{G}^{X}=\int_{t\in\mathcal{Q}^{X}}(G_{t})^{\hat{t}}
, where
(G_{t})^{\hat{t}}
denotes the group of maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
.
∎

We now show that gauge configurations and gauge transformations for a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
with classical defect data are determined uniquely by their components for strata of dimension
\geq n-1
.
For this we relate local
n
-strata
q:u\to t
and
(n-1)
-strata
x:u\to s
at a
k
-stratum
u
to paths in
Q^{X}
.

Recall from Lemma
3.20
that each local
l
-stratum
q:u\to v
can be represented by a sequence of local strata, each of which raises the dimension by one. The local strata in the sequence correspond to edges in
Q^{X}
and any such representation to a path in
Q^{X}
.
Recall from Definition
3.19
that such a representing sequence is characterised by the condition that the maps
S^{q}_{j}:S^{loc}_{j}(v)\to S^{loc}_{j}(u)

are given as the composites of the corresponding maps for the local strata in the sequence.

By Lemma
5.10
, for each local stratum
q:u\to v

that raises degree by one
, the components of gauge configurations and gauge transformations for
u
and
v
are related by the diagrams (
1
) and (
2
), which involve the maps
\iota_{q}:\hat{u}\to\hat{v}
from (
5.1
) and the maps
f_{q}:M_{u}\to M_{v}
and the group homomorphisms
\phi_{q}:G_{u}\to G_{v}
from (
4
).
We thus need to relate the maps
\iota_{r}
,
f_{r}
and group homomorphisms
\phi_{r}
for a local stratum
r
to the ones for local strata in its representing sequences.

Lemma 5.11
.

Let
s
be a
j
-stratum of a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
,
p:s\to t
a local
k
-stratum and
q:t\to u
and
r:s\to u
local
l
-strata such that
s\xrightarrow{p}t\xrightarrow{q}u
represents
r:s\to u
.
Then the associated maps from (
4
) and (
5.1
) satisfy

\iota_{r}=\iota_{q}\circ\iota_{p}:\hat{s}\to\hat{u},\qquad f_{r}=f_{q}\circ f_{p}:M_{s}\to M_{u},\qquad\phi_{r}=\phi_{q}\circ\phi_{p}:G_{s}\to G_{u}.

Proof.

The second and third identity follow directly from the definition of the maps
f_{x}:M_{v}\to M_{w}
and the group homomorphisms
\phi_{x}:G_{v}\to G_{w}
for a local stratum
x:v\to w
in (
4
), from (
4
) and from the identity
S_{j}^{r}=S_{j}^{p}\circ S_{j}^{q}
, which holds by definition of a representing sequence of local strata.

To see that the first identity holds, consider a triangulation
T
of
X
that contains all skeleta of the stratification as subcomplexes.
For a
j
-simplex
\hat{\sigma}
of
\hat{s}
,
k
-simplex
\hat{\tau}
of
\hat{t}
and
l
-simplex
\hat{\chi}
of
\hat{u}
, we denote by
\sigma=f_{s}(\hat{\sigma})
, by
\tau=f_{t}(\hat{\tau})
and
\chi=f_{u}(\hat{\chi})
the associated simplexes in
\bar{s}
,
\bar{t}
and
\bar{u}
.

Then
for each
j
-simplex
\hat{\sigma}
of
\hat{s}
and each
k
-simplex
\hat{\tau}
in
\hat{t}
such that
\tau
contains
\sigma
as a
j
-face and intersects a representative of
p
at a point
x\in\mathring{\sigma}
, the image
\iota_{p}(\hat{\sigma})
is the corresponding
j
-face of
\hat{\tau}
. Recall that all those
j
-faces correspond to a single
j
-simplex in
\hat{t}
.
Likewise, for each
k
-simplex
\hat{\tau}
of
\hat{t}
and each
l
-simplex
\hat{\chi}
in
\hat{u}
such that
\chi
contains
\tau
as a
k
-face and intersects a representative of
q
at a point
y\in\mathring{\tau}
, the image
\iota_{q}(\hat{\tau})
is the corresponding
k
-face of
\hat{\chi}
.

For each
j
-simplex
\hat{\sigma}
in
\hat{s}
and each
k
-simplex
\hat{\tau}
with
\iota_{p}(\hat{\sigma})\subset\hat{\tau}
, we can choose the point
y\in\mathring{\tau}
such that it is contained in a sufficiently small neighbourhood
U_{x}
of some point
x\in\mathring{\sigma}
as in
Definition
3.7
and in the proof of Lemma
3.17
.
The
j
-simplex
\iota_{q}\circ\iota_{p}(\hat{\sigma})
is then a
j
-face of an
l
-simplex
\hat{\chi}
in
\hat{u}
such that
\chi
contains
\sigma
as a
j
-face
and such that
\chi
intersects a representative of
p
at
y
. As
p
and
q
represent
r
at
y\in U_{x}
, this representative is also a representative of
r
at
x
.
Hence,
\iota_{q}\circ\iota_{p}(\hat{\sigma})=\iota_{r}(\hat{\sigma})
.
∎

Proposition 5.12
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data.

1.

A gauge configuration on
X
is an assignment

•

of a functor
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
to each
n
-stratum
t
,

•

of a map
h_{s}:\hat{s}\to M_{s}
to each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
,

such that for each path
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
and the inclusions
\iota_{l(s)}
,
\iota_{r(s)}
from Definition
3.5
and (
5.1
)

\displaystyle h_{s}(y^{\prime})=A_{L(s)}([\iota_{l(s)}\circ\delta])\rhd h_{s}(y)\lhd A_{R(s)}([\iota_{r(s)}\circ\delta])^{-1}.

(98)

2.

A gauge transformation on
X
is an assignment of a map
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
to each
n
-stratum
t
of
X
. It acts on a gauge configuration according to

\displaystyle A_{t}([\delta])\mapsto\gamma_{t}(x^{\prime})\cdot A_{t}([\delta])\cdot\gamma_{t}(x)^{-1}\qquad\qquad h_{s}(y)\mapsto\gamma_{L(s)}\circ\iota_{l(s)}(y)\rhd h_{s}(y)\lhd\gamma_{R(s)}\circ\iota_{r(s)}(y)^{-1}

(99)

for each
n
-stratum
t
,
(n-1)
-stratum
s
, path
\delta:x\to x^{\prime}
in
\hat{t}
and
y\in\hat{s}
.

Proof.

1. We use Lemma
5.10
to describe the functors
A_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\mathcal{D}_{u}
for strata
u
of dimension
\geq n-1
.

For each
n
-stratum
t
Lemma
5.10
gives just a functor
A_{t}=\psi_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
. For each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
, the group
G_{s}
is given by
G_{s}=G_{L(s)}\times G_{R(s)}
, where
l(s):s\to L(s)
and
r(s):s\to R(s)
are the two local
n
-strata at
s
.
The functors
D_{l(s)}:M_{s}\sslash G_{L(s)}\times G_{R(s)}\to\bullet\sslash G_{L(s)}
and
D_{r(s)}:M_{s}\sslash G_{L(s)}\times G_{R(s)}\to\bullet\sslash G_{R(s)}
send each element of
M_{s}
to
\bullet
and each morphism
(g_{L},g_{R}):m\to g_{L}\rhd m\lhd g_{R}^{-1}
to
g_{L}
and
g_{R}
, respectively.

The first diagram in (
1
) commutes trivially. The second diagram in (
1
) for
q=l(s)
and
q=r(s)
is equivalent to the condition

\psi_{s}([\delta])=(\psi_{L(s)}([\iota_{l(s)}\circ\delta]),\psi_{R(s)}([\iota_{r(s)}\circ\delta]))
for all paths
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
. This shows that
\psi_{s}
is determined uniquely by
\psi_{L(s)}
and
\psi_{R(s)}
. Condition (
79
) becomes condition (
98
).

2. We use the description of gauge transformations from Lemma
5.10
to describe the natural transformations
\gamma_{u}:A_{u}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{u}
for strata of dimension
\geq n-1
.

The natural transformation
\gamma_{t}:A_{t}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{t}
for an
n
-stratum
t
is simply a map
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
that satisfies
A^{\prime}_{t}([\delta])=\gamma_{t}(x^{\prime})\cdot A_{t}([\delta])\cdot\gamma_{t}(x)^{-1}
for all paths
\delta:x\to x^{\prime}
in
\hat{t}
. This gives the first formula in (
99
).

For an
(n-1)
-stratum
s
with local
n
-strata
l(s):s\to L(s)
and
r(s):s\to R(s)
the natural transformation
\gamma_{s}:A_{s}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{s}
is a map
\gamma_{s}:\hat{s}\to G_{L(s)}\times G_{R(s)}
that satisfies (
88
). Diagram (
2
) for
q=l(s)
and
q=r(s)
implies

\gamma_{L(s)}\circ\iota_{l(s)}=\pi_{L(s)}\circ\gamma_{s}
and
\gamma_{R(s)}\circ\iota_{r(s)}=\pi_{R(s)}\circ\gamma_{s}
. With (
88
) this yields the second formula in (
99
).

3. We show that every gauge configuration
A:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
is characterised uniquely by its components

for strata

of dimension
\geq n-1
.

We choose for each local

and each local

representing sequences

\displaystyle u=u_{k}\xrightarrow{x_{k+1}}u_{k+1}\to\ldots\to u_{n-2}\xrightarrow{x_{n-1}}u_{n-1}=s\qquad u=v_{k}\xrightarrow{q_{k+1}}v_{k+1}\to\ldots\to v_{n-1}\xrightarrow{q_{n}}v_{n}=t,

(100)

in which each local stratum raises the degree by one.
By Lemma
5.11
we then have

\displaystyle\iota_{x}=\iota_{x_{n-1}}\circ\ldots\circ\iota_{x_{k+1}}

\displaystyle f_{x}=f_{x_{n-1}}\circ\ldots\circ f_{x_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\phi_{x}=\phi_{x_{n-1}}\circ\ldots\circ\phi_{x_{k+1}}

(101)

\displaystyle\iota_{q}=\iota_{q_{n}}\circ\ldots\circ\iota_{q_{k+1}}

\displaystyle f_{q}=f_{q_{n}}\circ\ldots\circ f_{q_{k+1}}

\displaystyle\phi_{q}=\phi_{q_{n}}\circ\ldots\circ\phi_{q_{k+1}}

for the associated maps

).

(102)

Here, the maps
f_{x}:M_{u}\to M_{s}
and
\phi_{q}:G_{u}\to G_{t}
are simply the projection maps for the product.
By the universal property of the product, specifying the composites
f_{x}\circ h_{u}:\hat{u}\to M_{s}
for all local
(n-1)
-strata
x:u\to s
and the composites
\phi_{q}\circ\psi_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to G_{t}
for local
n
-strata
q:u\to t
determines the map
h_{u}:\hat{u}\to M_{u}
and the functor
\psi_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to G_{u}
from Lemma
5.10
. This shows that the functors
A_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\mathcal{D}_{u}
for any stratum
u
are uniquely determined by the maps
h_{s}:\hat{s}\to M_{s}
for
(n-1)
-strata
s
and functors
\psi_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
for
n
-strata
t
.

Conversely, given maps
h_{s}:\hat{s}\to M_{s}
for all
(n-1)
-strata
s
and functors
\psi_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
for all
n
-strata
t
that satisfy (
98
), we can define
the maps
h_{u}:\hat{u}\to M_{u}
and functors
\psi_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\bullet\sslash G_{u}
via (
102
). Identities (
4
), (
98
) and (

) then imply (
79
), which yields a functor
A_{u}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{u})\to\mathcal{D}_{u}
for each stratum
u
.
Identities
(
101
) and Lemma
5.11
then ensure that they combine into a natural transformation
A:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
.

4. We show that a gauge transformation is determined by its components
\gamma_{t}:A_{t}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{t}
for
n
-strata
t
.

Choose for each local
n
-stratum
q:u\to t
a representing sequence as in (
100
).
Then the map
\iota_{q}:\hat{u}\to\hat{t}
and group homomorphism
\phi_{q}:G_{u}\to G_{t}
are given as in (
101
), and combining the commuting diagrams (
2
) for the local strata
q_{j}
yields a corresponding diagram for
q
. This gives

\displaystyle\phi_{q}\circ\gamma_{u}=\gamma_{t}\circ\iota_{q}

(103)

for all local
n
-strata
q:u\to t
. As
\phi_{q}:G_{u}\to G_{t}
is the projection map for the product, this determines
\gamma_{u}
.

Conversely, given maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
for all
n
-strata
t
, we can define maps
\gamma_{u}:\hat{u}\to G_{u}
for all strata
u
by (
103
). Lemma
5.11
and identities (
4
), (
101
) and (
99
) then imply (
88
). This shows that the maps
\gamma_{u}:\hat{u}\to G_{u}

define a natural transformation
\gamma_{u}:A_{u}\Rightarrow A^{\prime}_{u}
.
Lemma
5.11
and
(
101
) then imply (
2
) for all local strata
q:u\to v
and yield a gauge transformation
\gamma:A\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}
.
∎

Proposition
5.12
shows again that the groupoid
G\big(Q^{X},T^{X},D^{X}\big)
of gauge configurations and gauge transformations indeed an action groupoid,
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
.
More specifically,
the group of gauge transformations
is the product of the groups of maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
over all
n
-strata
t
of
X

\mathcal{G}^{X}=\prod_{t\in S_{n}^{X}}(G_{t})^{\hat{t}}.

The functor
P_{\partial}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}
from Corollary
5.8
defines a map
P_{\partial}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\to\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}
and a group homomorphism

P_{\partial}:\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}
. The latter forgets the group elements for
n
-strata that are not associated with a boundary
(n-1)
-stratum. For
a boundary
(n-1)
-stratum
s
with associated

n
-stratum
t=\iota_{n-1}(s)
, the functor
P_{\partial}

restricts the map

\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
to
\gamma_{s}=\gamma_{t}\circ\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to G_{s}
, where
\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
is the map from (
5.1
).

Conversely, each boundary gauge transformation
\gamma^{\partial X}\in\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}
lifts to a gauge transformation
\gamma^{X}\in\mathcal{G}^{X}
with
P_{\partial}(\gamma^{X})=\gamma^{\partial X}
. The associated maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
for an
n
-stratum
t
of
X
assign the unit element of
G_{t}
to each point of
\hat{t}
that is not in the image of a map
\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
. For a point
x=\iota_{s\partial}(y)
,
they are given by
\gamma_{t}(x)=\gamma_{s}(y)
, as
\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
is injective. Hence,
P_{\partial}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}
is a fibration of groupoids.

If
M
is a stratified 3-manifold with boundary
\partial M=\Sigma_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}
and projection functors
P_{j}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{j}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{j}}
, then we have
\mathcal{A}^{\partial M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial M}=\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\times\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}}
, an isomorphism of categories and hence a fibration, and
P_{\partial}=\langle P_{0},P_{1}\rangle:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\times\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}}
. We obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 5.13
.

Any compact stratified 3-manifold
M
with classical defect data and boundary

\partial M=\Sigma_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}
defines a fibrant span of groupoids

\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\xleftarrow{P_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\xrightarrow{P_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}}.

This corollary and its derivation from Proposition
5.12
show that working with
fibrant
spans of groupoids is not merely a choice of technique, but has a direct gauge theoretical interpretation. The span of groupoids in Corollary
5.13
relates the gauge groupoid of
a cobordism
M:\Sigma_{0}\to\Sigma_{1}
to the gauge groupoids of its boundary surfaces
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
. That this span is fibrant corresponds to the fact that every boundary gauge transformation extends to a gauge transformation of the bulk.

5.4
Reduced gauge groupoids

For computations and examples, it is convenient to work with fundamental groupoids with chosen basepoints instead of full fundamental groupoids.
This requires a coherent choice of basepoints for all strata and a counterpart of Proposition
5.1
for fundamental groupoids with basepoints.

We denote by
\mathrm{Top}_{B}
the category whose objects are pairs
(X,U)
of a topological space
X
and a subspace
U\subset X
and whose morphisms
f:(X,U)\to(Y,V)
are continuous maps
f:X\to Y
with
f(U)\subset V
. The functor
\Pi_{1}:\mathrm{Top}_{B}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
sends a pair
(X,U)
to the full subgroupoid
\Pi_{1}(X,U)\subset\Pi_{1}(X)
with object set
U
and a morphism
f:(X,U)\to(Y,V)
to the functor
\Pi_{1}(f):\Pi_{1}(X,U)\to\Pi_{1}(Y,V)
.

To formulate a counterpart of Proposition
5.1
for fundamental groupoids with basepoints, one needs a choice of basepoints in
\hat{s}
for each stratum
s

that is compatible with the maps
\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
for local strata
q:s\to t
from (
5.1
) and with the maps
\iota_{s\partial}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
for boundary strata
s
from (
5.1
).

Definition 5.14
.

A
coherent basepoint set
for a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
is a finite set
B\subset X
that satisfies
V_{s}=f_{s}^{-1}(B)\neq\emptyset
for all strata and boundary strata
s
of
X
.

Note that the commuting diagrams (
5.1
) and (
5.1
) ensure that any coherent basepoint set
B\subset X
satisfies

•

\iota_{q}(V_{s})\subset V_{t}
for all
k
-strata
s
and local
(k+1)
-strata
q:s\to t
at
s
,

•

\iota_{s\partial}(V_{s})\subset V_{t}
for each boundary stratum
s
and associated
(k+1)
-stratum
t
from (
23
).

Example 5.15
.

For a compact
n
-manifold
X
with a fine stratification the set
B=X^{0}\cup\partial X^{0}
is a coherent basepoint set. The associated vertex sets

\displaystyle V_{s}=f_{s}^{-1}(X^{0}\cup\partial X^{0})\subset\hat{s},

(104)

are (i) non-empty by assumption, satisfy (ii) due to diagram (
5.1
) and (iii) due to diagram (
5.1
).

Proposition 5.16
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with a coherent basepoint set.

There is a functor
T^{X}_{\bullet}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
that sends

•

a
k
-stratum
s
to the groupoid
\Pi_{1}(\hat{s},V_{s})
,

•

a local
(k+1)
-stratum
q:s\to t
to the functor
\Pi_{1}(\iota_{q}):\Pi_{1}(\hat{s},V_{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})
,

and a natural transformation
\tau^{X\bullet}:T^{\partial X}_{\bullet}\Rightarrow T^{X}_{\bullet}I_{\partial}
with components

\displaystyle\tau^{X\bullet}_{s}=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{s\partial}):\Pi_{1}(\hat{s},V_{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t}).

(105)

The inclusion functors
\iota_{s}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{s},V_{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})
are equivalences of categories and define a natural transformation
\iota^{X}:T^{X\bullet}\Rightarrow T^{X}
such that the following diagram commutes

\textstyle{T^{\partial X\bullet}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{X\bullet}}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{\partial X}}
\textstyle{T^{\partial X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\tau^{X}}
\textstyle{T^{X\bullet}I_{\partial}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\iota^{X}I_{\partial}}
\textstyle{T^{X}I_{\partial}.}

Given a stratification with a coherent basepoint set, we obtain a reduced gauge groupoid by replacing the functor
T^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
from Proposition
5.1
by the functor

T^{X}_{\bullet}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
in Definition
5.7
.

Definition 5.17
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with a coherent basepoint set.
The
reduced gauge groupoid
on
X
is the groupoid

\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}=G(Q^{X},T^{X}_{\bullet},D^{X})
.

As in Proposition
5.12
one finds that this groupoid is an action groupoid and that reduced gauge configurations and reduced gauge transformations are characterised uniquely by their components for
n
- and
(n-1)
-strata.

Corollary 5.18
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
with defect data and a coherent basepoint set.

1.

A reduced gauge configuration on
X
is an assignment of

•

a functor
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
to each
n
-stratum
t
,

•

a map
h_{s}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
to each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
,

satisfying (
98
) for all
y,y^{\prime}\in V_{s}
and paths
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
.

2.

A reduced gauge transformation is an assignment of a map
\gamma_{t}:V_{t}\to G_{t}
to each
n
-stratum
t
that acts according to (
99
) for all paths
\delta:x\to x^{\prime}
in
\hat{t}
with
x,x^{\prime}\in V_{t}
and all
y\in V_{s}
.

We will now show that the reduced gauge groupoid of a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
is equivalent to its gauge groupoid. This will allow us to compute defect TQFTs by choosing coherent basepoint sets and working with reduced gauge groupoids. Moreover, it shows that all gauge groupoids are essentially finite.

Proposition 5.19
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data and a coherent basepoint set.
Then the groupoids
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
and
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
are equivalent.

Proof.

There is a canonical restriction functor
R:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
that sends

•

a gauge configuration
A:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
to
R(A)=A\circ\iota^{X}:T^{X\bullet}\Rightarrow D^{X}
,

•

a gauge transformation
\gamma:T^{X}\times I\Rightarrow D^{X}
to
R(\gamma)=\gamma\circ(\iota^{X}\times I):T^{X\bullet}\times I\Rightarrow D^{X}
,

where
\iota^{X}:T^{X\bullet}\Rightarrow T^{X}
is the natural transformation from Proposition
5.16
.
It restricts

•

the functors
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
to functors
A^{\bullet}_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
,

•

the maps
h_{s}:\hat{s}\to M_{s}
for each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
to maps
h^{\bullet}_{s}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
,

•

the maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
to maps
\gamma^{\bullet}_{t}:V_{t}\to G_{t}
.

To show that
R
is essentially surjective and fully faithful
we choose

•

for each
n
-stratum
t
and
x\in\hat{t}
a path
\alpha_{x}:v_{x}\to x
in
\hat{t}
with
v_{x}\in V_{t}
and
\alpha_{x}
constant for
x\in V_{t}
,

•

for each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
and
y\in\hat{s}
a path

\beta_{y}:v_{y}\to y
in
\hat{s}
with
v_{y}\in V_{s}
and
\beta_{y}
constant for
y\in V_{s}
.

1. To see that
R
is essentially surjective, we define for each
n
-stratum
t
and functor
A^{\bullet}_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
a functor
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
by setting for each path
\alpha:x\to y

A_{t}([\alpha])=A_{t}^{\bullet}([\alpha_{y}^{-1}\circ\alpha\circ\alpha_{x}]).

Note that this implies
A_{t}([\alpha_{x}])=1
for all
x\in\hat{t}
and
A_{t}([\alpha])=A_{t}^{\bullet}([\alpha])
for each path
\alpha:x\to y
with
x,y\in V_{t}
.

If
h_{s}^{\bullet}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
satisfies (
98
) for all paths
\beta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
with
y,y^{\prime}\in V_{s}
,
we obtain a map
h_{s}:\hat{s}\to M_{s}
that satisfies (
98
) for all paths
\beta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
by setting

\displaystyle h_{s}(y)

\displaystyle=A_{L(s)}([\iota_{l(s)}\circ\beta_{y}])\rhd h^{\bullet}_{s}(v_{y})\lhd A_{R(s)}([\iota_{r(s)}\circ\beta_{y}])^{-1},

where
\iota_{l(s)}
and
\iota_{r(s)}
are the maps from (
5.1
) for the local
n
-strata
l(s):s\to L(s)
and
r(s):s\to R(s)
from Definition
3.5
.
By construction, this yields
h_{s}(v)=h_{s}^{\bullet}(v)
for all
v\in V_{s}
.
Note also that
h_{s}(y)
does not depend on the choice of the path
\beta_{y}
, because
h_{s}^{\bullet}
satisfies (
98
) for paths with endpoints in
V_{s}
. This shows that for every gauge configuration
A^{\bullet}:T^{X\bullet}\Rightarrow D^{X}
there is a gauge configuration
A:T^{X}\Rightarrow D^{X}
with
R(A)=A^{\bullet}
.

2. To see that
R
is fully faithful, note that any gauge transformation
\gamma:A\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}
is given by maps
\gamma_{t}:\hat{t}\to G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
that satisfy

A^{\prime}_{t}([\alpha])=\gamma_{t}(y)\cdot A_{t}([\alpha])\cdot\gamma_{t}(x)^{-1}
for all paths
\alpha:x\to y
in
\hat{t}
. This implies

\displaystyle\gamma_{t}(x)=A^{\prime}([\alpha_{x}])\cdot\gamma_{t}(v_{x})\cdot A([\alpha_{x}])^{-1}

(106)

for all
x\in\hat{t}
. Thus, the maps
\gamma_{t}
are determined uniquely by the associated maps
R(\gamma)_{t}
and the functors
A_{t},A^{\prime}_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
. Hence, gauge transformations
\gamma:A\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}
are in bijection with gauge transformations
R(\gamma):R(A)\Rrightarrow R(A^{\prime})
.
This shows that
R
is fully faithful.
∎

Corollary 5.20
.

For each compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
of gauge configurations and gauge transformations on
X
is essentially finite.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.19
the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
is equivalent to the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
for each coherent basepoint set. It is thus sufficient to choose a coherent basepoint set and to show that
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
is finite.

By definition of a coherent basepoint set, all sets
V_{s}
for strata
s
of
X
are finite. Because all spaces
\hat{t}
for
n
-strata
t
of
X
are compact PL manifolds with boundary, their fundamental groups are finitely presented. For this note that a compact PL manifold with boundary has a finite triangulation. Contracting the 1-skeleton of such a triangulation gives a finite presentation of its fundamental group. With the finiteness of the groups
G_{t}
, this implies that the number of functors
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
is finite.
Due to the finiteness of the sets
M_{s}
assigned to the
(n-1)
-strata
s
, there is only a finite number of maps
h_{s}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
. This shows that
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
has only a finite number of objects. Likewise, the number of maps
\gamma_{t}:V_{t}\to G_{t}
is finite for each
n
-stratum
t
, which shows that all Hom-sets of
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
are finite.
∎

5.5
Geometrical and combinatorial description

If a compact
n
-manifold
X
is equipped with a fine stratification, it is possible to give a purely combinatorial description of the associated reduced gauge groupoid in terms of graph gauge configurations and graph gauge transformations on the thickening
X_{th}
. Note that in this case there are no isolated loops.

Recall from Table
1

that each (boundary) vertex of
X_{th}
is contained in a unique
n
-stratum
t
and (boundary) vertices of
X_{th}
in
t
are in bijection with elements of the set
V_{t}
from (
104
).

Recall also that the (boundary) edges of
X_{th}
are partitioned into two sets, black and red ones. Each black (boundary) edge of
X_{th}
is contained in a unique
n
-stratum
t
and corresponds to a unique (boundary) edge
e
of
X
with
e\subset\bar{t}
.
Red (boundary) edges of
X_{th}
are edges between a thickened vertex
v
and a thickened
(n-1)
-stratum
s
of
X
. They correspond to pairs
(v,q)
with a local
(n-1)
-stratum
q:v\to s
. Thus, the red edges in the thickening of an
(n-1)
-stratum
s
are in bijection with elements of the set
V_{s}
from (
104
).

Recall also that each (boundary) edge
e
of
X
with
e\subset\bar{s}
for an
(n-1)
-stratum
s
is thickened to a green rectangle
with two red edges
s(e),t(e)
at the start and target end and two black edges
l(e),r(e)
parallel to
e
that are contained in the
n
-strata
L(s)
,
R(s)
.
Each black and red edge is contained in a unique rectangle of this type.
For edges
e
of a 3-manifold
X
, the orientation of the black edges is fixed by the orientation of
e
. For boundary edges and edges of a surface
\Sigma
it is arbitrary. All red edges are oriented by the normals.

Definition 5.21
.

Let

1.

on

(ii)

m_{t(e)}=g_{l(e)}\rhd m_{s(e)}\lhd g_{r(e)}^{-1}
.

\displaystyle g_{l(e)}\mapsto g_{(t,l)}\cdot g_{l(e)}\cdot g_{(s,l)}^{-1}

A graph gauge configuration on the thickening

of a stratification is shown in Figure

If
X
is equipped with a fine stratification, then we can consider its coherent basepoint set from Example
5.15
and the associated groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
of reduced gauge configurations and gauge transformations from Definition
5.17
, which is equivalent to the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
by Proposition
5.19
. We then have

Corollary 5.22
.

Let
X
be a compact
n
-manifold with a fine stratification and classical defect data.
The groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X\bullet}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X\bullet}
is isomorphic to the groupoid of graph gauge configurations and transformations on
X_{th}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
5.19
a reduced gauge configuration is given by

•

a functor
A_{t}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{t},V_{t})\to\bullet\sslash G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
,

•

a map
h_{s}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
for each
(n-1)
-stratum
s
,

satisfying (
98
) for all
y,y^{\prime}\in V_{s}
and paths
\delta:y\to y^{\prime}
in
\hat{s}
.

A reduced gauge transformation is given by a map
\gamma_{t}:V_{t}\to G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
and acts on a reduced gauge configuration by (
99
) for each
y\in V_{s}
and path
\delta:x\to x^{\prime}
with
x,x^{\prime}\in V_{t}
.

As the stratification is fine, the thickening of an
n
-stratum
t
is an
n
-ball, whose boundary is a polyhedral
(n-1)
-sphere with vertex set
V_{t}
and the black edges in
t
as edges.
Each thickened
(n-1)
-stratum
s
is a polygonal cylinder with vertex set
V_{s}\times\{0,1\}
. Its red edges connect vertices
(v,0)
and
(v,1)
for
v\in V_{s}
. Its remaining edges are black edges shared with the thickenings of
L(s)
and
R(s)
.
This implies that reduced gauge configurations on
X
are in bijection with assignments

•

of group elements
g_{f}=A_{t}([f])
to each black edge
f
of
X_{th}
with
f\subset t
,

•

elements
m_{f}=h_{s}(v)
to each red edge
f
of
X_{th}
that connects
(v,0)
and
(v,1)
for
v\in V_{s}
.

Condition (
98
) then becomes condition (ii) in Definition
5.21
. The functoriality of
A_{t}
is equivalent to condition (i) in Definition
5.21

As vertices of
X_{th}
in an
n
-stratum
t
are in bijection with elements of
V_{t}
, a gauge transformation is simply an assignment of an element
g_{v}\in G_{t}
to each vertex of
X_{th}
contained in
t
. Formula (
99
) for the action of gauge transformation then becomes (
108
).
∎

Action of a gauge transformation for green planes in the thickening of an edge

.

The reduced counterparts of the restriction functors
P_{t}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{t}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})}
and
P_{\partial}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}
from Definition
5.7
and Corollary
5.8
also have a geometric interpretation.
They restrict the labels on edges and vertices of
X_{th}
to those edges and vertices that are contained in the thickening of a stratum
t
and in the boundary of
X_{th}
, respectively.
For a vertex
v
of
X
these are the red edges and vertices in its thickening. For an edge
e
these are the red edges at its starting and target vertex and the black edges that connect them.

It is possible to reduce the action groupoid for a compact
n
-manifold
X
with a fine stratification and classical defect data even further.
As all strata of
X
are balls, one can apply reduced gauge transformations to achieve that

A^{\bullet}_{t}([\alpha])=1
for all paths
\alpha:x\to y
in
\hat{t}
with
x,y\in V_{t}
and for all
n
-strata
t
. Condition (
98
) then states that the maps
h_{s}^{\bullet}:V_{s}\to M_{s}
are constant. This amounts to labelling all black edges of
X_{th}
by units of the associated groups and all red edges in the closure of an
(n-1)
-stratum
s
by the same element
m_{s}
. The reduced gauge transformations that preserve these restrictions are the ones given by constant maps
\gamma_{t}:V_{t}\to G_{t}
for each
n
-stratum
t
.
Applying Proposition
5.19
then yields

Corollary 5.23
.

Let
X
be a compact
n
-manifold with a fine stratification and classical defect data.
The groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
of gauge configurations and gauge configurations on
X
is equivalent to the action groupoid

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{0}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{0}=\big(\Pi_{s\in S^{X}_{n-1}}M_{s}\big)\sslash\big(\Pi_{t\in S^{X}_{n}}G_{t}\big).

(109)

This reduction procedure can also be understood geometrically. Considering only gauge configurations on
X_{th}
that assign trivial group elements to all black edges amounts to contracting all black edges in
X_{th}
. This contracts each thickened
n
-stratum to a point, each thickened
(n-1)
-stratum to an edge, each thickened
(n-2)
-stratum to a polygon and leaves the thickenings of
(n-3)
-strata unchanged. As a consequence, we obtain a graph with vertices corresponding to contracted
n
-strata and red edges dual to the
(n-1)
-strata of
X
. The normals of the
(n-1)
-strata equip this graph with an edge orientation.

Definition 5.24
.

Let
X
be a compact
n
-manifold with a fine stratification and classical defect data and
\Gamma
the 1-skeleton of its dual with the induced edge orientation.

1.

A
fully reduced graph gauge configuration
on
X
is an assignment of an element
m_{s}\in M_{s}
to the edge of
\Gamma
dual to an
(n-1)
-stratum
s
.

2.

A
fully reduced graph gauge transformation
on
X
is an assignment of an element
g_{t}\in G_{t}

to the vertex of
\Gamma
dual to an
n
-stratum
t
of
X
. It acts on a gauge configuration by

m_{s}\mapsto g_{L(s)}\rhd m_{s}\lhd g_{R(s)}^{-1}.

Clearly, fully reduced gauge configurations and gauge transformations on
X
form a groupoid, namely the action groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{0}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{0}
from Corollary
5.23
. We can then rephrase Corollary
5.23
as follows.

Corollary 5.25
.

Let
X
be a compact
n
-manifold with a fine stratification and classical defect data. Then the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}

of gauge configurations and gauge transformations on
X
is equivalent to the groupoid of fully reduced graph gauge configurations and transformations on
X
.

6
Construction of the defect TQFT

In this section we use the gauge groupoids of compact stratified
n
-manifolds with defect data to construct a defect TQFT.
We first show in Section
6.1
that the assignments of gauge groupoids to defect surfaces and defect cobordisms
define a symmetric monoidal functor
C:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
.

We then consider for each defect cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}M\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
the graph defined by its defect vertices and defect edges, with endpoints on the stratified surfaces
\Sigma_{0}
and
\Sigma_{1}
. We show in Section
6.2
that each defect edge
e
of
M
defines projection functors from the gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}
to the action groupoid
\mathcal{D}_{e}=M_{e}\sslash G_{e}
of the edge and natural transformations between them.
In Subsection
6.3
we then combine these natural transformations with the natural transformations at the defect vertices.
This defines a graph coloured by representations of the gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}
and intertwiners between them.

Its evaluation assigns to each surface
\Sigma_{j}
a functor
F_{\Sigma_{j}}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{j}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{j}}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
and to the cobordism
M
a natural transformation
\mu^{M}:F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}
, where
P_{i}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{i}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{i}}
are the projection functors from
Corollary
5.13
.
We then show that this defines a symmetric monoidal functor
G:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})
. Composing it with the symmetric monoidal functor
L:\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from Proposition
2.14
defines the defect TQFT.

6.1
Fibrant spans of groupoids from defect cobordisms

We consider for each stratified surface or cobordism
X
with classical defect data the associated gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}
from Definition
5.7
. We show that this assignment of gauge groupoids to stratified surfaces and cobordisms defines a symmetric monoidal functor from the defect cobordism category
\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}
from Definition
4.6
into the category
\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
from Lemma
2.7
. Recall from Corollary
5.20
that the gauge groupoid of any surface or cobordism is essentially finite and from Corollary
5.13
that a stratified compact 3-manifold
M
with classical defect data and boundary
\partial M\cong\Sigma_{0}\amalg\Sigma_{1}
defines a fibrant span
of essentially finite groupoids.

Theorem 6.1
.

There is a symmetric monoidal functor
C:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
, that assigns

•

to a stratified surface
\Sigma
with defect data its gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}
,

•

to a defect cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}M\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
the equivalence class of the fibrant span of groupoids

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\xleftarrow{P_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\xrightarrow{P_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}}.

Proof.

1. It is clear that
C
sends equivalent defect cobordisms to isomorphic and hence equivalent spans of essentially finite groupoids.
Although the functors
T^{\Sigma_{0}}
,
T^{\Sigma_{1}}
and
T^{M}
are defined by choosing a triangulation, they are triangulation-independent up to natural isomorphisms, as discussed in Subsection
5.1
.

2. We show that
C
respects identity morphisms:

We consider the cylinder cobordism
\Sigma\xrightarrow{\iota_{0}}\Sigma\times[0,1]\xleftarrow{\iota_{1}}\Sigma
. As
M:=\Sigma\times[0,1]
is equipped with the induced stratification, where each stratum
s
of
\Sigma
corresponds to a unique stratum
s\times[0,1]
of
M
,
we can identify
Q^{M}=Q^{\Sigma}
and take the insertions for both boundary components as
\mathrm{id}:Q^{\Sigma}\to Q^{M}
. We then have
D^{M}=D^{\Sigma}
.

By the construction in Section
5.1
, for each stratum
s
of
\Sigma
with associated space
\hat{s}
, the corresponding space for the stratum
s\times[0,1]
in
M
is given by
\hat{s}\times[0,1]
. As
\Pi_{1}:\mathrm{Top}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
preserves products,
we have
T^{M}=T^{\Sigma}\times\Pi_{1}[0,1]:\mathcal{Q}^{M}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
. The natural transformations
i^{j}:T^{\Sigma}\Rightarrow T^{\Sigma}\times\Pi_{1}[0,1]
have components
i^{j}_{s}=\mathrm{id}_{\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})}\times\{j\}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{s})\times\Pi_{1}[0,1]
for
j=0,1
. Using the fact that
\mathrm{Grpd}
is cartesian closed and that the functor
\mathrm{GRPd}(\mathcal{G},-):\mathrm{Grpd}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
is a right adjoint for any groupoid
\mathcal{G}
, we then obtain

\displaystyle\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}

\displaystyle=\int_{x\in Q^{\Sigma}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(T^{\Sigma}(x)\times\Pi_{1}[0,1],D^{\Sigma}(x)\big)\cong\int_{x\in Q^{\Sigma}}\mathrm{GRPd}\big(\Pi_{1}[0,1],\mathrm{GRPd}(T^{\Sigma}(x),D^{\Sigma}(x))\big)

\displaystyle\cong\mathrm{GRPd}\left(\Pi_{1}[0,1],\int_{x\in Q^{\Sigma}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{GRPd}(T^{\Sigma}(x),D^{\Sigma}(x))\right)=(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}.

As the projections
P_{j}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}
are induced by the natural transformations
\iota^{j}:T^{\Sigma}\Rightarrow T^{\Sigma}\times\Pi_{1}[0,1]
, they are identified with the functors

\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*},\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}:(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}\to(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})^{\bullet}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}
in (
10
). This shows that
C
sends the equivalence class of the cylinder cobordism to the equivalence class of the span from (
10
) in
\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
for
\mathcal{G}=\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}
.
By Lemma
2.10
this is the identity morphism in
\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
.

3. We show that
C
respects the composition of morphisms:

We glue cobordisms
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{f_{0}}M_{01}\xleftarrow{f_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
and
\Sigma_{1}\xrightarrow{f^{\prime}_{1}}M_{12}\xleftarrow{f_{2}}\Sigma_{2}
to a cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{j_{0}}M_{012}\xleftarrow{j_{2}}\Sigma_{2}
along the stratified surface
\Sigma_{1}
.
This amounts to gluing for each
k
-stratum
s_{1}
of
\Sigma_{1}
the associated
(k+1)
-strata
s_{01}
of
M_{01}
and
s_{12}
of
M_{12}
along
s_{1}
.

We denote by
Q_{j}:=Q^{\Sigma_{j}}
for
j=0,1,2
and
Q_{j}:=Q^{M_{j}}
for
j=01,12,012
the associated graded graphs and by
\mathcal{Q}_{j}
the associated free categories. By Lemma
3.23
, the maps
f_{0}
,

f_{1}
,
f^{\prime}_{1}
and
f_{2}
define insertions, which we denote by the same symbols.

Lemma 6.2
.

Gluing
M_{01}
and
M_{12}
along
\Sigma_{1}
yields the following commuting diagrams,
where the middle diamonds are pushouts:

3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{Q}_{012}.}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

All arrows in the first diagram are insertions, and in the second discrete opfibrations.

Proof.

The second diagram is obtained by applying the free category functor
L:\mathrm{Graph}\to\mathrm{Cat}
to the first diagram. As
a left adjoint, the functor
L:\mathrm{Graph}\to\mathrm{Cat}
preserves pushouts, and by Lemma
3.4
it sends insertions to discrete opfibrations. Hence, it is sufficient to prove that the middle diamond in the first diagram
is a pushout and all arrows are insertions.

The first statement follows, because the strata of the manifolds
M_{01}
and
M_{12}
are glued
along points
x\in\Sigma_{1}
to form the strata of
M_{012}
.
This shows
that the vertex set of the graph
Q_{012}
associated to
M_{012}
is indeed the pushout of the vertex sets of the graphs
Q_{01}
and
Q_{12}
along
Q_{1}
.

To show the corresponding statement for their edge sets, consider a stratum
s
of
\Sigma_{1}
.
Because all bundles of local strata are trivial, the sets of edges of
Q_{01}
,
Q_{12}
,
Q_{1}
and
Q_{012}
starting at
f_{1}(s)
,
f_{1}^{\prime}(s)
,
s
, and
j_{1}(s)
are all canonically identified with the sets of strata in any special neighbourhood of any point on these strata.
For any point
x\in s
, forming the pushout of special neighbourhoods
U_{01}
of
f_{1}(x)
in
M_{01}
and
U_{12}
of
f^{\prime}_{1}(x)
in
M_{12}

with
U_{01}\cap\partial M_{01}=f_{1}(U)
and
U_{12}\cap\partial M_{12}=f^{\prime}_{1}(U)
for a special neighbourhood
U
of
x
in
\Sigma_{1}
gives a special neighbourhood of
j_{1}(x)
in
M_{012}
. This shows that the diamond at the left of (
6.2
) is a pushout.

Consider a local
(k+1)
-stratum
q:s\to t
at a
k
-stratum
s
of
M_{012}
. If
s=j_{1}(s_{1})
for a
(k-1)
-stratum
s_{1}
of
\Sigma_{1}
, then, by construction,
q:s\to t
corresponds to a unique local
(k+1)
-stratum
q_{01}:f_{1}(s_{1})\to t_{01}
, a unique local
(k+1)
-stratum
q_{12}:f_{1}^{\prime}(s_{1})\to t_{12}
and a unique local
k
-stratum
q_{1}:s_{1}\to t_{1}
. If
j_{1}^{-1}(s)=\emptyset
, then
q
corresponds either to a unique local
(k+1)
-stratum
q_{01}:f_{1}(s_{1})\to t_{01}
or to a unique local
(k+1)
-stratum
q_{12}:f^{\prime}_{1}(s_{1})\to t_{12}
.
This shows that maps

j_{01}
,
j_{1}
and
j_{12}
are insertions.
As
f_{0},f_{1},f^{\prime}_{1},f_{2}
are insertions by assumption and composites of insertions are insertions, this also holds for the maps
j_{0}
and
j_{2}
.
∎

From (
6.2
) we then have the following diagrams of categories, functors and natural transformations

(122)

where

D_{j}:=D^{\Sigma_{j}},T_{j}:=T^{\Sigma_{j}}
for

and

for

. The natural transformations

and

are the ones from Proposition

.
The diagram on the right commutes. The arrows in the one on the left are related by natural transformations, as indicated in the diagram.
Applying Theorem

for
\mathbf{V}=\mathrm{Grpd}
yields the following commuting diagram, in which the middle diamond is a pullback

\textstyle{\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}_{0}}[T_{0},D_{0}]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}_{1}}[T_{1},D_{1}]}
\textstyle{\mathrm{End}_{\mathcal{Q}_{2}}[T_{2},D_{2}]}

where we abbreviate

The functor

in (

) is induced by the pushout from the commuting diagram (

) and the diagram on the right in (

)
and hence satisfies
D_{012}J_{01}=D_{01}
and

. The functor

We now use Definition

of the gauge groupoid and compare the definitions of

,

,

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}}

The functor

) and (

) coincides with the functor
D^{M_{012}}:\mathcal{Q}_{012}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
. This follows, because the defect data on the strata of

and

.

.

For this, we consider the functors
B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Top}
from Proposition

with

and abbreviate
B_{j}=B^{\Sigma_{j}}
for

and

for

. The natural transformations from Proposition

are denoted

and

. The counterparts of the

and

are

and

, given by (

) and (
