).

Lemma 6.3
.

The functor
B^{M_{012}}:\mathcal{Q}_{012}\to\mathrm{Top}
is the pushout in the functor category
\mathrm{Grpd}^{\mathcal{Q}_{012}}
of
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01}
and
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}
along the natural transformations
\mathcal{B}_{1}:\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}:\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}
,

B^{M_{012}}\cong\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01}\coprod_{\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}}\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}.

Proof.

Choose a triangulation of
M_{012}
that induces triangulations of
M_{01}
,
M_{12}
and
\Sigma_{0}
,
\Sigma_{1}
,
\Sigma_{2}
, such that all skeleta of the stratifications are subcomplexes.
By the construction in Section
5.1
, the space
\hat{s}
for each stratum
s
of
M_{012}
is
the pushout

\textstyle{\hat{s}}
\textstyle{\coprod_{s_{01}\in j_{01}^{-1}(s)}\hat{s}_{01}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\coprod_{s_{12}\in j_{12}^{-1}(s)}\hat{s}_{12}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\textstyle{\coprod_{s_{1}\in j_{1}^{-1}(s)}\hat{s}_{1},\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{B}_{1})_{s}=\langle\iota_{s_{1}\partial}\rangle}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1})_{s}=\langle\iota^{\prime}_{s_{1}\partial}\rangle}

where
\iota_{s_{1}\partial}=(\beta_{1})_{s_{1}}:\hat{s}_{1}\to\hat{s}_{01}
and
\iota^{\prime}_{s_{1}\partial}=(\beta^{\prime}_{1})_{s_{1}}:\hat{s}_{1}\to\hat{s}_{12}
are the components of the natural transformations
\beta_{1}:B_{1}\Rightarrow B_{01}F_{1}
and
\beta^{\prime}_{1}:B_{1}\Rightarrow B_{12}F^{\prime}_{1}
from Proposition
5.1
and
\langle\iota_{s_{1}\partial}\rangle
and
\langle\iota_{s_{2}\partial}\rangle
are induced by them via the universal properties of the coproducts.
This shows in particular that the maps
(\mathcal{B}_{1})_{s}
and
(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1})_{s}
are embeddings of subcomplexes for finite simplicial complexes.

By Lemma
6.2
, the functors
J_{1}
,
J_{01}
and
J_{12}
are discrete opfibrations. By comparing with Lemma
A.1
we can rewrite diagram (
6.1
) in terms of the functors
B^{X}:\mathcal{Q}^{X}\to\mathrm{Top}
from Proposition
5.1
as

\textstyle{B^{M_{012}}(s)}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{01}_{s}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}(s)\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{i^{12}_{s}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}(s).\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{B}_{1})_{s}}
\scriptstyle{(\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1})_{s}}

The map between the spaces
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}B_{x}(s)
and
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}B_{x}(t)
for
x=1,01,12
associated to a local stratum
q:s\to t
of
M_{012}

coincides with the ones in the commuting diagram
in Lemma
A.1
that describes the Kan extension on the morphisms. Combining the commuting diagrams (
6.1
) for a local stratum
q:s\to t
of
M_{012}
then yields
that the map
B^{M_{012}}(q)=\iota_{q}:\hat{s}\to\hat{t}
is induced by the universal property of this pushout.
∎

Lemma 6.4
.

The functor
T^{M_{012}}:\mathcal{Q}_{012}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
is given by the pushout

T^{M_{012}}=T_{012}\cong\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}T_{01}\!\!\!\!\coprod_{\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}T_{1}}\!\!\!\!\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}T_{12}

Proof.

By the proof of Lemma
6.3

the components of the natural transformations
\mathcal{B}_{1}:\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01}
and
\mathcal{B}^{\prime}_{1}:\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}\Rightarrow\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}
in (
6.1
) are embeddings of subcomplexes for a finite simplicial complex. Hence,
they are cofibrations. The Seifert van Kampen theorem for fundamental groupoids, as stated in
[
Br
, 9.1.2]
implies for each stratum
s
of
M_{012}
we have a pushout in
\mathrm{Grpd}

As colimits in functor categories are objectwise, this yields

T^{M_{012}}=\Pi_{1}B^{M_{012}}=(\Pi_{1}\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{01}}B_{01})\amalg_{\Pi_{1}\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{1}}B_{1}}(\Pi_{1}\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{12}}B_{12}).

It remains to show that
\Pi_{1}:\mathrm{Top}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
preserves the Kan extensions
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}B_{x}
for
x=1,01,12
, that is,

\Pi_{1}\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}B_{x}\cong\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}\Pi_{1}B_{x}
for
x=1,01,12
. As the maps
j_{x}:Q_{x}\to Q_{012}
are insertions by Lemma
6.2
, Lemma
A.1
implies
\mathrm{Lan}_{J_{x}}F_{x}(v^{\prime})=\amalg_{v\in j_{x}^{-1}(v^{\prime})}F_{x}(v)
for any functor
F_{x}:\mathcal{Q}_{x}\to\mathcal{C}
into a cocomplete category
\mathcal{C}
and any vertex
v^{\prime}
of
Q_{012}
. As
\Pi_{1}:\mathrm{Top}\to\mathrm{Grpd}
preserves coproducts, the claim follows.
∎

Thus, we have
T^{M_{012}}=T_{012}
. This allows us
to replace
\mathrm{End}_{Q_{012}}[T_{012},D_{012}]=\mathcal{A}^{M_{012}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M_{012}}
in diagram (
125
) and shows that
C
sends composites of cobordisms to the pullbacks of the associated spans.
Hence, we have a functor
C:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
. It is clear that
C
is symmetric monoidal.
∎

6.2
Projection functors for edges and natural transformations between them

As the second step in the construction of the defect TQFT, we investigate the groupoids associated with the defect edges of a compact stratified
n
-manifold
X
. For each defect edge
e
we construct projection functors to the groupoids
\mathcal{D}_{e}
and natural transformations between them.

Recall from Definition
5.7
that each stratum
s
of
X
gives rise to a functor,
P_{s}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}
.

Lemma 6.5
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data.

•

For each edge
e
and

we have a functor

.

•

For each edge
e

that is not an isolated loop, we have

24.94801pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.02573pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise \displaystyle(iv)\begin{gathered}\lx@xy@svg{\hbox{\raise 2.55554pt\hbox{\kern 26.33691pt\hbox{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\hbox{\vtop{\halign{\entry@#!@&&\entry@@#!@\cr&\cr\crcr}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern-22.02573pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.5466pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{t}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 30.79317pt\raise 5.92223pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-1.86113pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P_{\partial}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 50.33691pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\lx@xy@droprule}}{\hbox{\kern 50.33691pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 32.18121pt\raise-23.21225pt\hbox{{}\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise-2.74501pt\hbox{$\scriptstyle{P_{v}^{\partial X}}$}}}\kern 3.0pt}}}}}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\kern 21.15831pt\raise-24.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\lx@xy@tip{1}\lx@xy@tip{-1}}}}}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}\ignorespaces{\hbox{\lx@xy@drawline@}}{\hbox{\kern-26.33691pt\raise-32.93448pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 0.0pt\raise 0.0pt\hbox{\hbox{\kern 3.0pt\raise-2.55554pt\hbox{$\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}^{X}=\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\partial X},}$}}}}}}}\ignorespaces}}}}\ignorespaces\end{gathered}

(i)

if
e
is outgoing at a vertex
v
of
X
with associated local stratum
s(e):v\to e
,

(ii)

if
e
is incoming at a vertex
v
of
X
with associated local stratum
t(e):v\to e
,

(iii)

if
e
is outgoing at a boundary vertex
v
of
\partial X
,

(iv)

if
e
is incoming at a boundary vertex
v
of
\partial X
.

Proof.

For a stratum
t
of
X
or of
\partial X
we denote by
P_{t}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{t}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})}

or
P^{\partial X}_{t}:\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}\to\mathcal{D}_{t}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{t})}

the functor from Definition
5.7
.
The functor
R^{x}_{e}
for an edge
e
is then given as the composite

\displaystyle R^{x}_{e}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\xrightarrow{P_{e}}\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}\xrightarrow{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{x})^{*}}\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\bullet}\cong\mathcal{D}_{e},

(150)

where
\iota_{x}:\bullet\to\hat{e}
,
\bullet\mapsto x
.
If
e
is not an isolated loop, we identify
\hat{e}\cong[0,1]
and define

\displaystyle P^{s}_{e}=R^{0}_{e}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\xrightarrow{P_{e}}\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}\xrightarrow{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}}\mathcal{D}_{e}\qquad P^{t}_{e}=R^{1}_{e}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\xrightarrow{P_{e}}\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}\xrightarrow{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}}\mathcal{D}_{e}.

(151)

We also have
\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})=\Pi_{1}(\bullet)\cong\bullet
for a vertex
v
and canonical isomorphisms
\mathcal{G}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})}\cong\mathcal{G}
for each groupoid
\mathcal{G}
.

Cases (i) and (ii): If
v=s(e)
is the starting vertex of the edge
e
, the associated 1-stratum
q:v\to e
corresponds to the inclusion
\iota_{0}:\bullet\to[0,1]
, and if
v=t(e)
is the target vertex, it corresponds to the inclusion
\iota_{1}:\bullet\to[0,1]
. We then have
T^{X}(q)=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0}):\bullet\to\Pi_{1}[0,1]
for
v=s(e)
and and
T^{X}(q)=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1}):\bullet\to\Pi_{1}[0,1]
for
v=t(e)
.
The outer paths in the following diagram commute by definition of the end, and the inner vertical arrow is
P_{e}^{s}
for
v=s(e)
or
P_{e}^{t}
for
v=t(e)

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{v}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})}\cong\mathcal{D}_{v}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{D_{q}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}\cong\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}({\hat{v}})}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}.\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(q)^{*}}

(152)

Cases (iii) and (iv): If
e
is incident at a boundary vertex
v
,
the associated map
\iota_{v\partial}:\bullet\to[0,1]
from (
5.1
) is given by
\iota_{v\partial}=\iota_{0}:\bullet\to[0,1]
, if
v
is the starting vertex of
e
, and by
\iota_{v\partial}=\iota_{1}:\bullet\to[0,1]
, if
v
is the target vertex of
e
. Hence, the associated functor
\tau_{v}:\Pi_{1}(\hat{v})\to\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})
is given by
\tau_{v}=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})
and
\tau_{v}=\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})
, respectively. Corollary
5.8
and Remark
5.9
then yield commuting diagrams

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{t}}
\scriptstyle{P_{\partial}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}^{\partial X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}=\mathcal{D}_{v}}

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{s}}
\scriptstyle{P_{\partial}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\partial X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\partial X}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}^{\partial X}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{e}=\mathcal{D}_{v}.}

∎

Lemma 6.6
.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with classical defect data and
e
an edge of
X
.

•

Each path
\gamma:x\to y
in
\hat{e}
defines a natural transformation
H_{e}^{[\gamma]}:R_{e}^{x}\Rightarrow R_{e}^{y}
that depends only on the homotopy class of
\gamma
and satisfies for all paths
\delta:y\to z
in
\hat{e}

H^{[\delta]\cdot[\gamma]}_{e}=H_{e}^{[\delta]}\circ H^{[\gamma]}_{e}.

•

Each edge
e
that is incident to at least one vertex or boundary vertex defines

(i)

a unique natural transformation
P_{e}^{d}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
,

(ii)

for any
x\in\hat{e}
unique natural transformations
H^{0}_{x}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow R_{e}^{x}
and
H^{1}_{x}:R_{e}^{x}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
with
H^{1}_{x}\circ H^{0}_{x}=P_{e}^{d}
.

Proof.

The functors
R_{e}^{x},R_{e}^{y}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}
from Lemma
6.5
are given by (
150
).
We define

H_{e}^{[\gamma]}=\Pi_{1}([\gamma])^{*}P_{e}:R^{x}_{e}\Rightarrow R_{e}^{y}.

If
e
is incident at at least one vertex or boundary vertex
v
, we can identify
\hat{e}\cong[0,1]
. The functors
P_{e}^{s},P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}
from Lemma
6.5
are then given by (
151
). We choose paths
\delta:0\to 1
,
\delta_{0}:0\to x
and
\delta_{1}:x\to 1
in
[0,1]
and set

P_{e}^{d}=H^{[\delta]}_{e}
,
H_{x}^{0}=H_{e}^{[\delta_{0}]}
and
H_{x}^{1}=H_{e}^{[\delta_{1}]}
.
∎

6.3
Quantum defect data and the defect TQFT

We now use the projection functors and natural transformations from Section
6.2
and the assignment of defect data in Definition
4.4
to associate to each defect cobordism
M
a graph that is labelled with representations of the gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}
and intertwiners between them.

Let
X
be a compact stratified
n
-manifold with an assignment of defect data. Definition
5.7
assigns

•

to each edge
e
of
X
a functor
P_{e}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}^{\Pi_{1}(\hat{e})}
,

•

to each vertex
v
of
X
a functor
P_{v}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
.

Recall from Definition
4.4
that an assignment of quantum defect data assigns

•

to each codim 2-stratum
u
of
X
a representation
\rho_{u}:\mathcal{D}_{u}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
,

•

to each codim 3-stratum
v
an intertwiner
\sigma_{v}:\rho_{v}^{t}\Rightarrow\rho_{v}^{s}
, where
\rho_{v}^{s},\rho_{v}^{t}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
are given by (
47
) and
the groupoids
\mathcal{D}_{u}=D^{X}(u)
by Proposition
4.3
.

Lemma
6.5
and Lemma
6.6
assign

•

to each edge
e
of
X
that is not an isolated loop functors
P_{e}^{s},P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}
and a natural transformation
P_{e}^{d}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
,

•

to each isolated loop
e
of
X
and each
x\in\hat{e}
a functor
R_{e}^{x}:\mathcal{A}^{X}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{X}\to\mathcal{D}_{e}
and a natural transformation
H_{e}^{[\delta]}:R^{x}_{e}\Rightarrow R^{x}_{e}
, where
\delta:x\to x
is a path that goes once around
\hat{e}
in the direction of its orientation.

We assign to each stratified surface
\Sigma
with defect data a functor
F_{\Sigma}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
as follows. For a vertex
v
of
\Sigma
we set
F_{v}^{\Sigma}=\rho^{*}_{v}P_{v}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
, if
v
has negative orientation, and
F_{v}^{\Sigma}=\rho_{v}P_{v}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
, if
v
has positive orientation. We then take the tensor product of these functors over all vertices of
\Sigma

\displaystyle F_{\Sigma}=\bigotimes_{v\in S_{0}^{\Sigma}}F^{\Sigma}_{v}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}.

(153)

To each stratified cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
with defect data we will assign a natural transformation
\mu^{M}:F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}
, where
P_{j}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{j}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{j}}
is the projection functor from Corollary
5.13
. This natural transformation is constructed from a directed graph
\Gamma^{M}
, whose edges and vertices are coloured with representations of the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}
and intertwiners between them.

As the functor category
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}}
is spherical symmetric monoidal, cf. Section
2.1
, this defines a diagram for a spherical symmetric monoidal category, whose evaluation is an intertwiner between the representations
F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

The graph
\Gamma^{M}
is constructed as follows. We consider the 1-skeleton of the stratification and add to each edge
e
of
M
a bivalent vertex
v_{e}
. For an isolated loop
e
, we also choose a vertex
\hat{v}_{e}\in\hat{e}
with
f_{e}(\hat{v}_{e})=v_{e}
.
This replaces each edge of
M
that is not an isolated loop by a pair of edges corresponding to its starting and target end. All edges created in this way are equipped with the induced orientations. The open edge ends of
\Gamma^{M}
correspond to the boundary vertices of
M
.

We assign to the directed graph
\Gamma^{M}
the following data:

•

to each edge
e
of
\Gamma^{M}
that is outgoing at a vertex or boundary vertex
v
of
M
the functor

\displaystyle F_{e}^{s}:=\rho_{e}P_{e}^{s}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}},

(154)

•

to each edge
e
of
\Gamma^{M}
that is incoming at a vertex or boundary vertex
v
of
M
the functor

\displaystyle F_{e}^{t}:=\rho_{e}P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}},

(155)

•

to each edge of
\Gamma^{M}
that arises from an isolated loop, the functor defined by (
150
)

\displaystyle F^{\hat{v}_{e}}_{e}:=\rho_{e}R_{e}^{\hat{v}_{e}}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}},

(156)

•

to a vertex of
\Gamma^{M}
that arises from a vertex
v
of
M
the natural transformation

\displaystyle\mu^{v}:=\sigma_{v}P_{v}:\rho_{v}^{t}P_{v}\Rightarrow\rho_{v}^{s}P_{v},

(157)

•

to each new bivalent vertex
v_{e}
of
\Gamma^{M}
that is not on an isolated loop the
natural transformation

\displaystyle\mu^{e}:=\rho_{e}P_{e}^{d}:F_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow F_{e}^{t},

(158)

•

to each bivalent vertex
v_{e}
on an isolated loop
the natural transformation

\displaystyle\rho_{e}H_{e}^{[\delta]}:F_{e}^{\hat{v}_{e}}\Rightarrow F_{e}^{\hat{v}_{e}}

(159)

for a path
\delta:\hat{v}_{e}\to\hat{v}_{e}
that circles
\hat{e}
once in the direction of its orientation.

This defines a graph
\Gamma^{M}_{col}
coloured by objects and morphisms in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}^{\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}}
.
Each directed graph with such a labelling
defines an equivalence class of diagrams for this symmetric monoidal category.

They are obtained by embedding the coloured graph in
\mathbb{R}^{3}
and choosing a generic projection
\pi:\mathbb{R}^{3}\to[0,1]\times[0,1]
, such that any singular point is a transversal crossing of two edges and the open edge ends end on
[0,1]\times\{1\}
or
[0,1]\times\{0\}
. We call the former incoming and the latter outgoing edge ends. As a consequence of the coherence theorem for spherical categories and the symmetric monoidality, two diagrams obtained in this way evaluate to the same morphism, whenever their sets of incoming and of outgoing edges coincide and have the same linear ordering.
Different choices of linear orderings for the edge ends lead to morphisms related by permutations of tensor factors in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
, and different choices of incoming and outgoind edge ends are related by taking duals in
\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
. The relevant information is the structure of a directed graph.

For our defect cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}

we choose the open ends corresponding to vertices in
\Sigma_{0}
as the incoming ends in the diagram and the open ends for vertices of
\Sigma_{1}
as the outgoing ends. Each vertex
u
of the boundary
\partial M
is incident to a unique 1-stratum
e_{u}
of
M
. We put
x_{u}=s
if
e_{u}
is outgoing at
u
and

x_{u}=t
if
e_{u}
is incoming at
u
. For a vector space
V
we put
V^{u}=V
, if
u\in\Sigma_{0}
with negative orientation or if
u\in\Sigma_{1}
with positive orientation, and set
V^{u}=V^{*}
otherwise. Note that negative orientation corresponds to an outgoing and positive orientation to an incoming stratum
e_{u}
at
u
.

The coloured graph
\Gamma^{M}_{col}
then evaluates to the natural transformation

\mu^{M}:=\langle\Gamma^{M}_{col}\rangle:\bigotimes_{v\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{0}}}\left(F_{e_{v}}^{x_{v}}\right)^{v}\Rightarrow\bigotimes_{u\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{1}}}\left(F_{e_{u}}^{x_{u}}\right)^{u}.

With formula (
153
) and
Corollary
5.8
we obtain

\displaystyle\bigotimes_{v\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{0}}}\left(F_{e_{v}}^{x_{v}}\right)^{v}=\bigotimes_{v\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{0}}}F_{v}^{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}=F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0},\qquad\qquad\bigotimes_{u\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{1}}}\left(F_{e_{u}}^{x_{u}}\right)^{u}=\bigotimes_{u\in S_{0}^{\Sigma_{1}}}F_{u}^{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}=F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1},

which shows that the evaluation of
\Gamma^{M}_{col}
is a natural transformation

\displaystyle\mu^{M}=\langle\Gamma^{M}_{col}\rangle:F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}.

(160)

Although we selected a point
\hat{v}_{e}\in\hat{e}
for each isolated loop
e
to construct the labels of
\Gamma^{M}_{col}
, its evaluation does not depend on this choice. If
\hat{v}_{e},\hat{v}^{\prime}_{e}\in\hat{e}
are two choices of vertices with associated loops
\delta:\hat{v}_{e}\to\hat{v}_{e}
and
\delta^{\prime}:\hat{v}^{\prime}_{e}\to\hat{v}^{\prime}_{e}
, then their natural transformations from Lemma
6.6
are related by

H^{[\delta^{\prime}]}_{e}=H^{[\gamma]}_{e}\circ H^{[\delta]}_{e}\circ H^{[\gamma]^{-1}}_{e}

for any path
\gamma:\hat{v}_{e}\to\hat{v}^{\prime}_{e}
in
\hat{e}
. As the evaluation of an isolated loop is just the trace, it is independent of
\hat{v}_{e}
.

Proposition 6.7
.

There is a symmetric monoidal functor

G:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})
that assigns

•

to a stratified surface the pair
(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma},F_{\Sigma})
, where
F_{\Sigma}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
is the functor from (
153
),

•

to a cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
the pair
(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\xleftarrow{P_{0}}\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\xrightarrow{P_{1}}\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}},\mu^{M})
of the fibrant span assigned by Theorem
6.1
and the natural transformation
\mu^{M}:F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}
from (
160
).

Proof.

That the classical defect data defines a functor
G:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
was already shown in Theorem
6.1
. It remains to show that

1.

the gluing of cobordisms induces the composition of natural transformations,

2.

the cylinder cobordism defines the identity natural transformation.

1. Let

\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M_{01}\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
, and

\Sigma_{1}\xrightarrow{i_{1}^{\prime}}M_{12}\xleftarrow{i_{2}}\Sigma_{2}
be stratified cobordisms and

\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{j_{1}}M\xleftarrow{j_{2}}\Sigma_{2}
their composite with
M=M_{01}\amalg_{\Sigma_{1}}M_{12}
. By Theorem
6.1
this
yields the following diagram, where the top two levels commute and the top diamond is a pullback

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{01}}
\scriptstyle{P}
\scriptstyle{P_{12}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{M_{01}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M_{01}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{0}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\,\,\,\mu^{M_{01}}}
\scriptstyle{P_{1}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{M_{12}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M_{12}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{1}^{\prime}}
\scriptstyle{P_{2}}
\textstyle{\scriptstyle\,\,\,\mu^{M_{12}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{0}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{0}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{\Sigma_{0}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{\Sigma_{1}}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{2}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{2}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{F_{\Sigma_{2}}}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}.}

We show that the natural transformations for the cobordisms from (
160
) satisfy
\mu^{M}=(\mu^{M_{12}}P_{12})\circ(\mu^{M_{01}}P_{01})
.

Let
\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col}
and
\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col}

be the coloured graphs associated to
M_{01}
and
M_{12}
and
\mu^{M_{01}}=\langle\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col}\rangle
and
\mu^{M_{12}}=\langle\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col}\rangle
their natural transformations from (
160
). Pre-composition with
P_{01}
and
P_{12}
equips the underlying graphs with a colouring by the groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}
and defines coloured graphs
\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}
and
\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}
with

\mu^{M_{01}}P_{01}=\langle\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\rangle\qquad\textrm{ and }\qquad\mu^{M_{12}}P_{12}=\langle\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}\rangle.

Denoting by
\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\#\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}
their concatenation along their edge ends in
\Sigma_{1}
, we then have

(\mu^{M_{12}}P_{12})\circ(\mu^{M_{01}}P_{01})=\langle\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\rangle\circ\langle\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}\rangle=\langle\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\#\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}\rangle.

The coloured graph
\Gamma^{M}_{col}
is obtained from
\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\#\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}
in two steps. One first erases all bivalent vertices on the edges of
M_{01}
and
M_{12}
that intersect
\Sigma_{1}
. One then adds a bivalent vertex to all edges of the resulting new graph that now have no bivalent vertex.
This bivalent vertex is then labelled by (i) the natural transformation from
(
158
)
, if the resulting edge is incident to at least one vertex or boundary vertex, or (ii) by the natural transformation from (
159
), if it is an isolated loop. We then have

\displaystyle\mu^{M}=\langle\Gamma^{M}_{col}\rangle=\langle\Gamma^{M_{01}}_{col*}\#\Gamma^{M_{12}}_{col*}\rangle.

(161)

This follows, because for each point
x\in S^{1}
the homotopy class of a path based at
x
that goes once around
S^{1}
in the direction of its orientation is the composite of the homotopy classes of paths obtained by subdividing
S^{1}
into a finite number of intervals. An analogous statement holds for the homotopy classes
[\gamma]:0\to 1
of paths in
[0,1]
. Applying the identities in Lemma
6.6
then gives (
161
).

2. Let
M=\Sigma\times[0,1]
be the cylinder cobordism with a single
(k+1)
-stratum
s\times[0,1]
for each
k
-stratum
s
of
\Sigma
.
Then the coloured graph for
M
consists of a single line with a bivalent vertex
v_{e}
for each vertex
v
of
\Sigma
. For each vertex
v
of
\Sigma
and associated edge
e=v\times[0,1]
of
\Sigma\times[0,1]
the two ends of this line are labelled with functors
F_{e}^{s}=\rho_{v}P_{e}^{s}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
and
F_{e}^{t}=\rho_{v}P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
. The vertex
v_{e}
in the middle is labelled with the natural transformation
\mu^{e}=\rho_{v}P_{e}^{d}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
from (
158
).

We then have for each edge
e=v\times[0,1]
of
M
the groupoid
\mathcal{D}_{e}=\mathcal{D}_{v}=M_{v}\sslash G_{v}
. As
e
is incident at two boundary vertices, we can identify
\hat{e}\cong[0,1]
and the inclusions for the boundary vertices from (
5.1
) are given by
\iota_{0},\iota_{1}:\bullet\to[0,1]
.

The functors

P_{e}^{s},P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
from Definition
6.5
send a gauge configuration
A:T^{M}\Rightarrow D^{M}
to
A_{e}(0),A_{e}(1)\in M_{v}
and a gauge transformation
\gamma:A\Rrightarrow A^{\prime}
to the group elements
\gamma_{e}(0),\gamma_{e}(1)\in G_{v}
with
\gamma_{e}(0)\rhd A_{e}(0)=A^{\prime}_{e}(0)
and
\gamma_{e}(1)\rhd A_{e}(1)=A^{\prime}_{e}(1)
, respectively.

The natural transformation
P_{e}^{d}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
from Lemma
6.6
has components
(P_{e}^{d})_{A}=A_{e}([\delta]):A_{e}(0)\to A_{e}(1)
, where
\delta:0\to 1
is a path in
[0,1]
. The equivalence
\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\cong\mathrm{GRPd}(\Pi_{1}[0,1],\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})
from the proof of Theorem
6.1
identifies

•

the
functors
P_{e}^{s},P_{e}^{t}:\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
with the functors
P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*},P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathcal{D}_{v}
induced by the inclusions
\iota_{0},\iota_{1}:\bullet\to[0,1]
,

•

the
natural transformation
P^{d}_{e}:P_{e}^{s}\Rightarrow P_{e}^{t}
with
P_{v}[\delta]^{*}:P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}\Rightarrow P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}
induced by the homotopy class of any path
\delta:0\to 1
in
[0,1]
.

This gives the following commuting diagram

\textstyle{\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\cong}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{s}}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow P_{e}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{P_{e}^{t}}
\textstyle{{\mathcal{D}}_{v}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{(}A^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow[\delta]^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}}
\textstyle{(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})^{\bullet}\cong\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}}
\scriptstyle{P_{v}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\Pi_{1}[0,1]}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Downarrow[\delta]^{*}}
\scriptstyle{\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}}
\textstyle{\mathcal{D}_{v}^{\bullet}\cong\mathcal{D}_{v}.}

Composing with
\rho_{e}=\rho_{v}:\mathcal{D}_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
then yields
\mu^{e}=\rho_{v}P_{v}[\delta]^{*}:\rho_{v}P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}\Rightarrow\rho_{v}P_{v}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}
.
Taking the tensor product over all edges
e
of
M
gives
\mu^{M}=\langle\Gamma^{M}_{col}\rangle=F_{\Sigma}[\delta]^{*}:F_{\Sigma}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{0})^{*}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma}\Pi_{1}(\iota_{1})^{*}
, and combining this result with Lemma
2.10
proves the claim.
∎

By composing the functor
G:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})
from Proposition
6.7
with the symmetric monoidal functor
L:\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from Proposition
2.14
we obtain a defect TQFT.

Theorem 6.8
.

The symmetric monoidal functor
\mathcal{Z}=LG:\mathrm{Cob}^{\mathrm{def}}_{3}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
is a defect TQFT.

We will now show how ordinary Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT without defects is recovered from this defect TQFT by considering surfaces and cobordisms with only
codim
0
-strata.

We first consider defect surfaces and defect cobordisms with only codim
\leq 1
strata. Such defect surfaces and defect cobordisms form a symmetric monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}\subset\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}
. For any defect surface
\Sigma
in this subcategory the associated functor
F_{\Sigma}:\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from (
153
) is the constant functor with values in
\mathbb{C}
. For any defect cobordism
M
in this subcategory the associated natural transformation
\mu^{M}:F_{\Sigma_{0}}P_{0}\Rightarrow F_{\Sigma_{1}}P_{1}
from (
160
) is the identity natural transformation on the constant functor.

By Remark
2.9
such representations and intertwiners form the monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})\subset\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})
. The restriction of the functor
L:\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
to this subcategory is the symmetric monoidal functor
L_{\mathrm{class}}:\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from Corollary
2.15
. We thus obtain a commuting diagram of functors, where the vertical arrows are inclusions

\textstyle{\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{repGrpd})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{\hskip 16.38895ptL}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}.}
\textstyle{\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{G_{\mathrm{class}}\hskip 16.38895pt}
\textstyle{\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces\ignorespaces}
\scriptstyle{L_{\mathrm{class}}}

Corollary 6.9
.

The restriction of the functor
\mathcal{Z}:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from Theorem
6.8
to the monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}\subset\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{def}}
assigns

•

to a surface
\Sigma
the vector space
\mathcal{Z}^{H}(\Sigma)=\langle\pi_{0}(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma})\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}
,

•

to the equivalence class of a cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
the linear map
\mathcal{Z}^{H}(M):\mathcal{Z}^{H}(\Sigma_{0})\to\mathcal{Z}^{H}(\Sigma_{1})
with matrix elements

\big\langle[A_{1}]\mid\mathcal{Z}^{H}(M)\mid[A_{0}]\big\rangle=\chi^{\pi}\big(\mathrm{PC}_{A_{1}}(\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma_{1}}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma_{1}})\big)\cdot\chi^{\pi}\big(\{A_{0}\mid\mathcal{A}^{M}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{M}\mid A_{1}\}\big).

Here, the notation is as in Corollary
2.15
.
This defect TQFT with only codim
\leq 1
strata was investigated by Fuchs, Schweigert and Valentino in
[
FSV2
]
in the more general case of twisted Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT, where 2-strata and 3-strata are equipped with cocycles. The TQFT from Corollary
6.9
agrees with the results in
[
FSV2
]
, if the latter are restricted to trivial cocycles.
In particular, the graphical calculus for group cocycles in
[
FSV2
, Section 3.4]
is also defined in the case where all group cocycles are trivial, and then reduces to our diagrammatic description from Section
5.5
.

Ordinary Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT without defects corresponds to defect surfaces and defect cobordisms with only codim
0
-strata. Such defect surfaces and defect cobordisms have as defect data a single finite group
H
for each codim 0-stratum. This defines for each finite group
H
a symmetric
monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Cob}^{H}_{3}\subset\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}
, in which all 0-strata are labelled with
H
. The restriction of the defect TQFT from
Corollary
6.9
to this subcategory is given by Quinn’s finite total homotopy TQFT
[
Q
,
FMP2
]
for the classifying space of a finite group. This is precisely Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT with a trivial cocycle.
The latter is given by the formulas in Corollary
6.9
and the following corollary. This follows from
[
FMP2
, §8.2]
and
[
Mo
]
.

Corollary 6.10
.

For any finite group
H
, the restriction of
G_{\mathrm{class}}:\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}\to\mathrm{span}^{\mathrm{fib}}(\mathrm{Grpd})
to the monoidal subcategory
\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{H}\subset\mathrm{Cob}_{3}^{\mathrm{class}}
assigns

•

to a surface
\Sigma
the essentially finite groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash G^{\Sigma}=(\bullet\sslash H)^{\Pi_{1}(\Sigma)}
,

•

to the equivalence class of a cobordism
\Sigma_{0}\xrightarrow{i_{0}}M\xleftarrow{i_{1}}\Sigma_{1}
the fibrant span of essentially finite groupoids

(\bullet\sslash H)^{\Pi_{1}(\Sigma_{0})}\xleftarrow{P_{0}=\Pi_{1}(i_{0})^{*}}(\bullet\sslash H)^{\Pi_{1}(M)}\xrightarrow{P_{1}=\Pi_{1}(i_{1})^{*}}(\bullet\sslash H)^{\Pi_{1}(\Sigma_{1})}.

The resulting TQFT

\mathcal{Z}^{H}:\mathrm{Cob}^{H}_{3}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
coincides with Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT for the finite group
H
.

Note that in this case a coherent basepoint set is obtained by choosing a single basepoint on each connected component of a surface
\Sigma
and a single basepoint on each connected component of the boundary
\partial M
of a cobordism
M
. For a connected surface
\Sigma
this yields the reduced gauge groupoid
\mathcal{A}^{\Sigma}\sslash\mathcal{G}^{\Sigma}=\mathrm{Hom}(\pi_{1}(\Sigma),H)\sslash H
, where
H
acts by conjugation.

7
Examples

In this section, we illustrate our formalism and the resulting defect TQFTs with examples. We first give some examples of defect data in Section
7.1
.
In Section
7.2
we then compute examples of the two-dimensional part of a defect TQFT, the vector spaces assigned to surfaces.
Section
7.3
treats examples of defect cobordisms.

The results in Section
7.2
are of independent interest in topological quantum computing. The vector space a Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT assigns to a surface
is the ground state of Kitaev’s quantum double model
[
K
]
and also describes the closely related Levin-Wen model
[
LW
]
. Our defect TQFT gives a simple description of quantum double models with defects that can be viewed as a special case of the models introduced by Kitaev and Kong
[
KK
]
. In our formalism, the ground state of the quantum double model with defects can be computed efficiently and without choosing a triangulation.

7.1
Defect data

To treat examples of defect TQFTs we require some preliminaries about defect data. First, we introduce a notion of transparent defect that corresponds to the absence of defects.

Definition 7.1
.

Let
X
be a stratified
n
-manifold with defect data.

1.

A
transparent
codim 1-stratum is labelled with a group
G
as a
G\times G^{op}
-set.

2.

A
transparent
codim 2-stratum on a codim 1-stratum labelled with a
G\times H^{op}
-set
M
is labelled with

\displaystyle\rho_{M}:M\times M\sslash G\times H\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}\qquad\rho_{M}(m,m^{\prime})=\begin{cases}\mathbb{C}&m=m^{\prime}\\
0&m\neq m^{\prime}\end{cases}\qquad\rho_{M}(g,h)=\mathrm{id}.

(162)

3.

A
transparent
codim 3-stratum on a codim 2-stratum with
\rho
is labelled
with
\mathrm{id}_{\rho}:\rho\Rightarrow\rho
.

We will also need non-transparent representations of action groupoids and intertwiners between them.
For this, we relate them to modules over smash products of group algebras and their intertwiners.
For a finite set
M
we denote by
\mathbb{C}^{M}
the algebra of maps
f:M\to\mathbb{C}
with the pointwise multiplication and with the basis given by the maps
\delta_{m}:M\to\mathbb{C}
with
\delta_{m}(n)=0
for
n\neq m
and
\delta_{m}(m)=1
.
If
\rhd:G\times M\to M
is an action of a finite group
G
, then
\mathbb{C}^{M}
becomes a module algebra over the Hopf algebra
\mathbb{C}[G]
with the induced
\mathbb{C}[G]
-action given by
g\rhd\delta_{m}=\delta_{g\rhd m}
on the basis. The associated smash product
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
is the associative unital algebra
\mathbb{C}[G]\otimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
with the multiplication

\displaystyle(g\otimes\delta_{m})\cdot(h\otimes\delta_{n})=\delta_{m}(h\rhd n)\,gh\otimes\delta_{n}.

(163)

We denote by
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}\mathrm{-Mod}
the category of its finite-dimensional representations and intertwiners.

Lemma 7.2
.

For any action
\rhd:G\times M\to M
of a finite group
G
on a finite set
M
the categories

\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}^{M\sslash G}
and
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}\mathrm{-Mod}
are equivalent.

Proof.

The equivalence is given by the functor
F:\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}^{M\sslash G}\to\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}\mathrm{-Mod}
that sends

•

a functor
\rho:M\sslash G\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
to the vector space
V_{\rho}=\oplus_{m\in M}\rho(m)
with the
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
-module structure

\displaystyle(g\otimes\delta_{m})\rhd v=\iota_{g\rhd m}\circ\rho(g)\circ\pi_{m}(v),

•

a natural transformation
\mu:\rho\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}
to the intertwiner

\displaystyle\phi_{\mu}=\Sigma_{m\in M}\,\iota^{\prime}_{m}\circ\mu_{m}\circ\pi_{m}:\oplus_{m\in M}\rho(m)\to\oplus_{m\in M}\rho^{\prime}(m),

and the functor
H:\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}\mathrm{-Mod}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}^{M\sslash G}
that sends

•

a
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
-module
(V,\rhd)
to the functor
\rho:M\sslash G\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
given on the objects by

\rho(m)=(1\otimes\delta_{m})\rhd V
and on a morphism
g:m\to g\rhd m
by
\rho(g)=(g\otimes\delta_{m})\rhd-:\rho(m)\to\rho(g\rhd m)
,

•

an intertwiner
\phi:V\to V^{\prime}
to the natural transformation
\mu:\rho\Rightarrow\rho^{\prime}
whose components are the (co)restrictions of
\phi
to the subspaces
V_{m}=(1\otimes\delta_{m})\rhd V
and
V^{\prime}_{m}=(1\otimes\delta_{m})\rhd^{\prime}V^{\prime}
.

∎

To construct representations of an action groupoid
M\sslash G
it is then sufficient to construct representations of the smash product
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
. The construction of irreducible representations of such smash products is analogous to the construction of irreducible representations of the Drinfeld double
D(G)
by Gould
[
G
]
.

Consider a
G
-orbit
\mathcal{O}
on
M
and a representative
m_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
with stabiliser
N=\mathrm{Stab}(m_{0})
. Fix for each element
m\in\mathcal{O}
an element
h_{m}\in G
with
h_{m}\rhd m_{0}=m
such that
h_{m_{0}}=1
. For each representation
\sigma:N\to\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathbb{C}}(V)
consider the induced representation of
G
on
\mathbb{C}[G]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[N]}V
. For any basis
B
of
V
the elements
h_{m}\otimes v
for
m\in\mathcal{O}
and
v\in B
form a basis of
\mathbb{C}[G]\otimes_{\mathbb{C}[N]}V
, and its

\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
-module structure is given by

\displaystyle(g\otimes\delta_{n})\rhd(h_{m}\otimes v)=\delta_{n}(m)\;h_{g\rhd m}\otimes\sigma(h_{g\rhd m}^{-1}gh_{m})v.

(164)

It is easy to see that this module is irreducible if and only if
\sigma
is irreducible. Thus,
G
-orbits on
M
and irreducible representations of their stabilisers define irreducible representations of
\mathbb{C}[G]\ltimes\mathbb{C}^{M}
.
By combining this result with Lemma
7.2
one obtains functors
\rho:M\sslash G\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
.

Corollary 7.3
.

Let
\rhd:G\times M\to M
be an action of a finite group
G
on a finite set
M
. Let
\mathcal{O}
a
G
-orbit with a representative
m_{0}\in\mathcal{O}
and
h_{m}\in G
with
h_{m}\rhd m_{0}=m
for each
m\in\mathcal{O}
such that
h_{m_{0}}=1
.
Then any representation
\sigma:\mathrm{Stab}(m_{0})\to\mathrm{Aut}_{\mathbb{C}}(V)
defines a functor
\rho_{\sigma}:{M\sslash G}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
with

•

\rho_{\sigma}(m)=V
for each
m\in\mathcal{O}
and
\rho_{\sigma}(m)=0
for
m\notin\mathcal{O}
,

•

\rho_{\sigma}(g)(v)=\sigma(h^{-1}_{g\rhd m}gh_{m})v
for a morphism
g:m\to g\rhd m
,
m\in\mathcal{O}
, and
\rho(g)=\mathrm{id}_{0}
if
m\notin\mathcal{O}
.

Example 7.4
.

1.

Let
G\sslash G
the action groupoid for the conjugation action of a finite group
G
on itself. Then the smash product (
163
) is the (untwisted) Drinfeld double
D(G)
and the irreducible representations in (
164
) coincide with the irreducible representations of
D(G)
constructed in
[
G
]
, see
[
W
]
.

2.

Consider a
G
-set
M
and the
G
-set
M\times M
with the diagonal action. The
G
-orbits
\mathcal{O}_{m}=G\rhd(m,m)
for
m\in M
with the trivial stabiliser representations yield the transparent representation from (
162
).

7.2
Defect surfaces and generalised quantum double models

In this section, we show that the vector space a defect TQFT assigns to a closed surface
\Sigma
can be viewed as a generalisation of the ground state of Kitaev’s quantum double model for a finite group
G
. This model was introduced in
[
K
]
as a realistic model for a topological quantum computer.

It takes as algebraic data a
finite group
G
. Its topological data is a directed graph
\Gamma\subset\Sigma
embedded into a closed oriented surface
\Sigma
such that
\Sigma\setminus\Gamma
is a disjoint union of discs. For simplicity, we take
\Sigma
to be connected.
In our setting,
\Gamma
is the dual graph of a fine stratification of
\Sigma
. We denote by

V
,
E
and
P
the sets of vertices, edges and plaquettes of
\Gamma
.
We say that a plaquette is
n
-valent if its dual vertex has
n
incident edge ends.

The quantum double model associates to this data the complex vector space
\mathcal{H}=\otimes_{e\in E}\mathbb{C}[G]=\mathbb{C}[G^{\times E}]
, together with two families of linear endomorphisms of

\mathcal{H}
. The first family is associated with vertices
v
of
\Sigma
and indexed by group elements
g\in G
. It describes the action of graph gauge transformations at
v

A^{g}_{v}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H},\quad h_{e}\mapsto g_{t(e)}\cdot h_{e}\cdot g_{s(e)}^{-1},

where
h_{e}
denotes the group element assigned to an edge
e\in E
,
g_{v}=g
and
g_{w}=1
for
w\neq v
.
The second family of endomorphisms is associated with plaquettes
p\in P
together with a choice of a vertex
v_{p}
in
p
and also indexed by elements
g\in G
. It assigns to an element of
G^{\times E}
the value 1 if the ordered oriented product
h_{p}
of group elements at the edges of
p
starting at
v_{p}
satisfies
h_{p}=g
and
0
otherwise

B^{g}_{p}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H},\quad x\mapsto\delta_{g}(h_{p})\,x.

For any site
s=(v_{p},p)
, a pair of a plaquette
p
and a chosen vertex
v_{p}
at
p
, the maps
A_{v}^{g}
and
B^{h}_{p}
for
g,h\in G
form a representation of the Drinfeld double
D(G)
on
\mathcal{H}
. In terms of the parametrisation (
163
) it is given by

(g\otimes\delta_{h})\rhd x=A_{v}^{g}B_{p}^{h}x.

The ground state of the model corresponds to the trivial representation of
D(G)
at each site of
\Gamma
. The maps

A_{v}=|G|^{-1}\Sigma_{g\in G}A_{v}^{g}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}\qquad B_{p}=B_{p}^{1}:\mathcal{H}\to\mathcal{H}

for
v\in V
and
p\in P
are commuting projectors, and the ground state of the quantum double model on
\Sigma
is

\displaystyle\mathcal{H}_{inv}=\left(\cap_{v\in V}\mathrm{im}(A_{v})\right)\cap\left(\cap_{p\in P}\mathrm{im}(B_{p})\right)\cong\langle\mathrm{Hom}(\pi_{1}(\Sigma),G)/G\rangle_{\mathbb{C}}.

(165)

It was shown by Balsam and Kirillov
[
BK2
]
in the more general setting of a finite-dimensional semisimple Hopf algebra
H
that the ground state coincides with the vector space the Turaev-Viro-Barrett-Westbury TQFT for
H\mathrm{-Mod}
assigns to
\Sigma
. In our setting this is the free vector space generated by conjugacy classes of group homomorphisms
\rho:\pi_{1}(\Sigma)\to G
, assigned to
\Sigma
by the untwisted Dijkgraaf-Witten TQFT for
G
.

It is also shown in
[
BK2
]
that excitations in this model are given by irreducible representations of the Drinfeld double
D(H)
. In our situation this is the Drinfeld double
D(G)
given by
(
163
) for the conjugation action.
Excitations are associated with sites
s=(v,p)
and form irreducible representations of
D(G)
as in Corollary
7.3
and Example
7.4
, 1. They are given by a conjugacy class
\mathcal{C}\subset G
and a representation of a stabiliser.

We will now show how the groupoid
\mathcal{A}\sslash\mathcal{G}
of gauge configurations and gauge transformations on a stratified surface
\Sigma
, the associated functor
F_{\Sigma}:\mathcal{A}\sslash\mathcal{G}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from (
153
) and the vector space

\mathcal{Z}(\Sigma)=\lim F_{\Sigma}
generalise the quantum double model. More specifically, they define a quantum double model with excitations, domain walls and domain wall junctions that can be viewed as a concrete and accessible manifestation of the more general models considered by Kitaev and Kong
[
KK
]
.

We consider a fine stratification of
\Sigma
whose dual is the directed graph
\Gamma
. Then the framing of the stratification assigns to each plaquette
p
of
\Gamma
a vertex
v_{p}
.
By Definition
4.4
a labelling with defect data assigns

•

a finite group
G_{v}
to each vertex
v
of
\Gamma
,

•

a finite
G_{t(e)}\times G_{s(e)}^{op}
-set
M_{e}
to each edge
e
of
\Gamma
,

•

a functor
\rho_{p}:\Pi_{e\in p}M_{e}\sslash\Pi_{v\in p}G_{v}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
to each plaquette
p
of
\Gamma
.

Here, the products are taken over all edges and vertices in
p
, taking into account their multiplicities. A set
M_{e}
or a group
G_{v}
occurs as often as the path along the boundary of
p
traverses
e
and
v
.

By Corollary
5.25
the groupoid of gauge configurations and gauge transformations is equivalent to the groupoid of graph gauge configurations and graph gauge transformations on
\Gamma

\mathcal{A}\sslash\mathcal{G}=\left(\Pi_{e\in E}M_{e}\right)\sslash\left(\Pi_{v\in V}G_{v}\right).

•

A graph gauge configuration assigns an element
m_{e}\in M_{e}
to each edge
e
of
\Gamma
.

•

A graph gauge transformation assigns an element
g_{v}\in G_{v}
to each vertex
v
of
\Gamma
and acts by

m_{e}\mapsto g_{t(e)}\rhd m_{e}\lhd g_{s(e)}^{-1}.

The functor
F_{\Sigma}:\mathcal{A}\sslash\mathcal{G}\to\mathrm{Vect}_{\mathbb{C}}
from (
153
) sends a gauge configuration to the tensor product of all vector spaces associated to the labelled plaquettes of
\Gamma
and a gauge transformation to a linear map between them. The defect TQFT assigns to
\Sigma
the vector space

\mathcal{Z}(\Sigma)=\lim F_{\Sigma}.

To make contact with quantum double models, we consider the following specific cases of plaquettes, their action groupoids
\mathcal{A}\sslash\mathcal{G}
and the associated representations

:

, where

if the oriented ordered product of its group elements is trivial and else the null vector space. For instance,
